// Package game - Ability Effect Execution
// Implements the resolution of triggered and activated ability effects
package game

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"math/rand"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

// exileFromBattlefield moves a permanent from the battlefield directly to
// its owner's exile zone, mirroring exileFromHand/exileFromGraveyard's
// RemoveCard/AddCard/EventExiled shape (activated_abilities.go) for the
// battlefield zone specifically.
func (gs *GameState) exileFromBattlefield(card *Card) {
	if card == nil {
		return
	}
	gs.Battlefield.RemoveCard(card.ID) //nolint:errcheck
	owner := card.Owner
	if owner < 0 || owner >= len(gs.Players) {
		owner = card.Controller
	}
	if owner < 0 || owner >= len(gs.Players) {
		return
	}
	if gs.Players[owner].Exile != nil {
		gs.Players[owner].Exile.AddCard(card)
	}
	if gs.EventBus != nil {
		gs.EventBus.Publish(NewEvent(EventExiled).WithSource(card).WithPlayer(owner))
	}
}

// executeExileTargetPlusAttachedEquipment implements the
// exile_target_plus_attached_equipment primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-target-plus-attached-equipment-3bbeb6bde5, Soul Nova
// class): "Exile target attacking creature and all Equipment attached to
// it." Mirrors executeBounceAttachedAndItsAuras's discovery shape exactly
// — every Equipment currently attached to the targeted creature is found
// by scanning the battlefield for AttachedTo == targetCreatureID at
// resolution time, so the attached-Equipment set is dependent on which
// creature was targeted, NOT a battlefield-wide Equipment filter
// (negative example) — but sends both the Equipment and the anchor to
// EXILE (via exileFromBattlefield above) instead of hand/graveyard.
// Equipment is exiled BEFORE the targeted creature, same anchor-last
// ordering executeBounceAttachedAndItsAuras uses. A missing/already-gone
// target creature is a no-op, matching every other partial-legality
// executor in this file; exiling only the creature without also exiling
// its attached Equipment (the other negative example) never happens
// since both are always resolved together in this single function.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileTargetPlusAttachedEquipment(targetCreatureID string) error {
	if targetCreatureID == "" {
		return nil
	}
	creature, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(targetCreatureID)
	if err != nil || creature == nil {
		return nil
	}
	equipment := []*Card{}
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || c.AttachedTo != targetCreatureID {
			continue
		}
		isEquipment := false
		for _, st := range c.SubTypes {
			if st == "Equipment" {
				isEquipment = true
				break
			}
		}
		if !isEquipment {
			continue
		}
		equipment = append(equipment, c)
	}
	for _, equip := range equipment {
		gs.exileFromBattlefield(equip)
	}
	gs.exileFromBattlefield(creature)
	return nil
}

// DealDamageSameNameAsTargetCreature implements the
// damage_same_name_as_target_creature primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-damage-same-name-as-target-creature-ff183ed4e9, Homing
// Lightning class): "~ deals N damage to target creature and each other
// creature with the same name as that creature." Unlike a plain
// single-target damage effect, every OTHER battlefield creature whose
// Name exactly matches the chosen target's Name (Rule 201.4 — object
// names compare as printed) is ALSO dealt the same amount, one
// CreateDamageEvent/ProcessTriggeredAbilities pair per creature actually
// hit (the same event plumbing shape_dealt_damage_test.go exercises for
// receiver-side "is_dealt_damage" triggers) — so per-creature triggers
// see one damage event per creature, not a single shared event for the
// whole group. No-op if the target isn't a card or isn't found on the
// battlefield, mirroring the no-target no-op convention used throughout
// this file.
func (gs *GameState) DealDamageSameNameAsTargetCreature(source *Card, controller int, target Target, amount int) error {
	if !target.IsCard || amount <= 0 {
		return nil
	}
	var targetCard *Card
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c != nil && c.ID == target.CardID {
			targetCard = c
			break
		}
	}
	if targetCard == nil {
		return nil
	}
	name := targetCard.Name
	gs.ProcessTriggeredAbilities(CreateDamageEvent(source, targetCard, amount, controller))
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || c.ID == targetCard.ID || c.Name != name {
			continue
		}
		gs.ProcessTriggeredAbilities(CreateDamageEvent(source, c, amount, controller))
	}
	return nil
}

// executeExileTwoCardsFromGraveyardUpkeepCost implements the
// exile_cards_from_graveyard primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-cards-from-graveyard-11177f64d7, Aegis Sculptor class):
// "At the beginning of your upkeep, you may exile two cards from your
// graveyard. If you do, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature." This pays
// only the optional-cost half of that clause — exiling exactly two cards
// from the ability CONTROLLER's own graveyard (never a battlefield
// permanent or any other zone) — and reports whether the cost was paid so
// a caller can gate its own "if you do" follow-up, the same may/if-you-do
// shape executeExileFromGraveyardEffect (Kroxa/Kunoros class) already
// uses. Rule 601.2h: a player can't choose to pay a cost they can't fully
// pay, so with fewer than two cards in the graveyard nothing is exiled and
// this returns false.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileTwoCardsFromGraveyardUpkeepCost(controller int) bool {
	player := gs.GetPlayer(controller)
	if player == nil {
		return false
	}
	cards := player.Graveyard.GetAll()
	if len(cards) < 2 {
		return false
	}
	for i := 0; i < 2; i++ {
		removed, err := player.Graveyard.RemoveCard(cards[i].ID)
		if err != nil {
			continue
		}
		player.Exile.AddCard(removed)
		if gs.EventBus != nil {
			gs.EventBus.Publish(NewEvent(EventExiled).WithSource(removed).WithPlayer(controller))
		}
	}
	return true
}

// DealDamageEqualToCounterCount implements the damage_amount_counter_count
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-damage-amount-counter-count-82dee1907b,
// Falkenrath Exterminator class): "~ deals damage to target creature equal
// to the number of +1/+1 counters on this creature." Unlike the literal-
// amount and map_atom-resolved damage shapes (which pre-fold a plain int
// before dispatch), the amount here is read directly off counterSource's
// own Counters AT RESOLUTION time (Rule 608.2g) — counterSource is
// whichever permanent the ability's text actually references (usually the
// source itself, as in the blocked example, but kept as its own parameter
// so a future reference to some OTHER permanent isn't precluded). Returns
// the resolved amount alongside dispatching the damage event so callers
// can observe exactly what got dealt. No-op (amount 0, nil error) if
// counterSource is nil, the target isn't a card, the target isn't found
// on the battlefield, or counterSource currently has none of the named
// counter type — mirroring the amount<=0 no-op convention used throughout
// this file (see DealDamageSameNameAsTargetCreature above).
func (gs *GameState) DealDamageEqualToCounterCount(source *Card, controller int, target Target, counterSource *Card, counterType CounterType) (int, error) {
	if counterSource == nil || !target.IsCard {
		return 0, nil
	}
	amount := counterSource.Counters.GetCount(counterType)
	if amount <= 0 {
		return 0, nil
	}
	var targetCard *Card
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c != nil && c.ID == target.CardID {
			targetCard = c
			break
		}
	}
	if targetCard == nil {
		return 0, nil
	}
	gs.ProcessTriggeredAbilities(CreateDamageEvent(source, targetCard, amount, controller))
	return amount, nil
}

// executeDestroyLowestManaValueNonlandPermanentEffect implements the
// destroy_lowest_mana_value_nonland_permanent primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-destroy-lowest-mana-value-nonland-permanent-cd5217d89c,
// Culling Scales class): "destroy target nonland permanent with the
// lowest mana value. (If two or more permanents are tied for lowest,
// target any one of them.)" Scans the whole battlefield, skipping every
// land (Culling Scales' filter names only NONland permanents — a land's
// mana value is irrelevant and it is never a candidate regardless of
// how it compares), and keeps the first permanent seen whose
// ManaCost.CMC() is strictly lower than the best found so far. Since
// the printed reminder text says ties resolve to "any one of them," the
// first minimum found is destroyed with no further tiebreak rule —
// mirrors executeMenaceCounterOnControlledCreature's first-match,
// no-tiebreak convention just below. No-op if the battlefield has no
// nonland permanents.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyLowestManaValueNonlandPermanentEffect() error {
	var lowest *Card
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || c.IsLand() {
			continue
		}
		if lowest == nil || c.ManaCost.CMC() < lowest.ManaCost.CMC() {
			lowest = c
		}
	}
	if lowest == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return DestroyTarget(Target{IsCard: true, CardID: lowest.ID}, gs)
}

// executeExileGreatestPowerCreature implements the filter_greatest_power_creature
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-filter-greatest-power-creature-204e0f8b6e, Topple
// class): "Exile target creature with the greatest power among creatures on the
// battlefield. (If two or more creatures are tied for greatest power, target any
// one of them.)" Mirrors executeDestroyLowestManaValueNonlandPermanentEffect
// above: scans the whole battlefield (creatures only this time — a nonland
// permanent filter would be wrong here), keeping the first creature seen whose
// effective power (BasePower plus any continuous-effect bonus from
// EffectManager.GetPowerBonus, the same read combat math already relies on) is
// strictly GREATER than the best found so far, then exiles it directly
// (Battlefield removal + EffectManager.RemoveBySource cleanup + a move into the
// owner's Exile zone) instead of destroying it. Since the printed reminder text
// says ties resolve to "any one of them," the first maximum found is exiled
// with no further tiebreak rule, matching the no-tiebreak convention used by
// the lowest-mana-value sibling above. No-op if the battlefield has no
// creatures.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileGreatestPowerCreature(controller int) error {
	var best *Card
	bestPower := 0
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || !c.IsCreature() {
			continue
		}
		power := 0
		if c.BasePower != nil {
			power = *c.BasePower
		}
		if gs.EffectManager != nil {
			power += gs.EffectManager.GetPowerBonus(c, c.Controller)
		}
		if best == nil || power > bestPower {
			best = c
			bestPower = power
		}
	}
	if best == nil {
		return nil
	}
	gs.Battlefield.RemoveCard(best.ID)
	if gs.EffectManager != nil {
		gs.EffectManager.RemoveBySource(best)
	}
	owner := best.Owner
	if owner < 0 || owner >= len(gs.Players) {
		owner = controller
	}
	gs.Players[owner].Exile.AddCard(best)
	return nil
}

// executeDestroyTargetIfManaValueEqualsUnspentMana implements the
// conditional_destroy_by_dynamic_numeric_compare primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-conditional-destroy-by-dynamic-numeric-compare-e3e6fddab6,
// Glissa Sunseeker class): "Destroy target artifact if its mana value is
// equal to the amount of unspent mana you have." Unlike the unconditional
// single-target destroy primitive, this checks a numeric equality between
// the candidate target's mana value (Card.ManaCost.CMC(), the same read
// executeDestroyLowestManaValueNonlandPermanentEffect above uses) and a
// dynamic, controller-owned state value (the activating controller's
// current unspent mana, unspentManaTotal below) immediately before
// destroying — mirroring executeCounterIfControllerPoisoned's per-target
// gate-then-act shape. If the comparison is false the target is simply
// skipped (the destroy fizzles, matching Rule 608.2b — nothing else about
// the target is affected); if true it destroys through the same
// DestroyTarget helper every other destroy primitive in this file uses.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyTargetIfManaValueEqualsUnspentMana(controller int, targets []Target) error {
	unspent := gs.unspentManaTotal(controller)
	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		var card *Card
		for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
			if c != nil && c.ID == target.CardID {
				card = c
				break
			}
		}
		if card == nil || card.ManaCost.CMC() != unspent {
			continue
		}
		if err := DestroyTarget(target, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeReturnFromGraveyardManaValueX implements the
// return_from_graveyard_mana_value_x primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-from-graveyard-mana-value-x-3344aa7f88, Immortal
// Servitude class): "Return each creature card with mana value X from
// your graveyard to the battlefield." X is resolved from the spell's
// paid value by the ExecuteAbilityEffect / ApplySpellEffectWithRuntime
// dispatch chokepoints (dynamic_amount.go) and arrives pre-folded into
// effectValue["amount"] — the same key executeSearchLibraryXAmountToHand
// (effects.go) reads for its own X-based effect. Every creature card in
// the controller's own graveyard whose mana value (Card.ManaCost.CMC(),
// the same read executeDestroyLowestManaValueNonlandPermanentEffect
// above uses — NOT a nonexistent Card.ManaValue field) exactly equals X
// returns to the battlefield under its owner's control. Matches are
// collected before mutating the graveyard so removal doesn't skip
// entries while iterating a shrinking zone.
func (gs *GameState) executeReturnFromGraveyardManaValueX(controller int, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	x := 0
	switch v := effectValue["amount"].(type) {
	case int:
		x = v
	case float64:
		x = int(v)
	}
	var matches []*Card
	for _, c := range gs.Players[controller].Graveyard.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || !c.IsCreature() {
			continue
		}
		if c.ManaCost.CMC() != x {
			continue
		}
		matches = append(matches, c)
	}
	for _, c := range matches {
		gs.Players[controller].Graveyard.RemoveCard(c.ID)
		c.Owner = controller
		c.Controller = controller
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(c)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeReturnSpecificCardFromGraveyardToHand implements the
// auto_return_from_graveyard_to_hand primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-from-graveyard-to-hand-ac4db7aa6f, Sigil of the New
// Dawn class): "return that card to your hand" — where "that card" is a
// SPECIFIC, already-identified graveyard card (e.g. the creature named by
// a "whenever a creature is put into your graveyard" trigger), as opposed
// to an auto-selected or player-targeted filter match. Deliberately
// distinct from the battlefield-return primitives (this ticket's negative
// example is "return a creature from the battlefield to its owner's
// hand") — only the graveyard is searched. Looks the card up by ID across
// every player's graveyard (a card's owner, not the ability's controller,
// is whose zones it lives in per Rule 400.3) and moves it straight to
// that same player's hand. No-op if the card is not currently in any
// player's graveyard (e.g. it was already moved by an earlier effect in
// the same resolution).
func (gs *GameState) executeReturnSpecificCardFromGraveyardToHand(cardID string) error {
	for _, p := range gs.Players {
		if c, err := p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(cardID); err == nil {
			p.Hand.AddCard(c)
			return nil
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeReturnFromGraveyardManaValueFilter implements the
// return_from_graveyard_mana_value_filter primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-from-graveyard-mana-value-filter-dc7c776e15, Pre-War
// Formalwear class): "return target creature card with mana value 3 or
// less from your graveyard to the battlefield ..." Unlike
// executeReturnFromGraveyardManaValueX above (a graveyard-wide sweep for
// EXACT mana value X) or executeReturnCreatureAndLandFromGraveyard (TYPE
// filter only, to hand), this is a single explicitly-TARGETED graveyard
// card returning to the BATTLEFIELD, gated by an optional card_type
// filter (effectValue["card_type"], e.g. "creature", matching
// Card.IsCreature()) AND an optional mana-value-or-less threshold
// (effectValue["mana_value_max"], compared against Card.ManaCost.CMC()).
// Omitting mana_value_max preserves plain card_type-only filtering
// unchanged — the ticket's explicit negative example. A target that is
// missing from the graveyard or fails either filter is skipped rather
// than failing the whole effect (Rule 601.2c: an illegal individual
// target doesn't invalidate the others).
func (gs *GameState) executeReturnFromGraveyardManaValueFilter(controller int, targets []Target, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	cardType, _ := effectValue["card_type"].(string)
	hasMaxMV := false
	maxMV := 0
	switch v := effectValue["mana_value_max"].(type) {
	case int:
		maxMV = v
		hasMaxMV = true
	case float64:
		maxMV = int(v)
		hasMaxMV = true
	}
	graveyard := gs.Players[controller].Graveyard
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := graveyard.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		if cardType == "creature" && !card.IsCreature() {
			continue
		}
		if hasMaxMV && card.ManaCost.CMC() > maxMV {
			continue
		}
		removed, err := graveyard.RemoveCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil {
			continue
		}
		removed.Owner = controller
		removed.Controller = controller
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(removed)
	}
	return nil
}

// unspentManaTotal returns the total amount of mana currently sitting in
// controller's mana pool (Rule 106.4), for the dynamic-state-comparison
// primitives above. Rather than reading ManaPool's per-color fields
// directly, this probes ManaPool.CanPay/Pay's own generic-cost payment
// logic (card.go's CastWithPhyrexianMana/CastWithHybridMana comments) with
// an increasing all-color-eligible {N} cost — since a generic cost can be
// paid with mana of any color, the largest N for which CanPay(ManaCost{
// Generic: N}) still holds true equals exactly the pool's total unspent
// mana, regardless of which colors it's actually made of.
func (gs *GameState) unspentManaTotal(controller int) int {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return 0
	}
	pool := gs.Players[controller].ManaPool
	total := 0
	for pool.CanPay(ManaCost{Generic: total + 1}) {
		total++
		if total > 200 {
			break
		}
	}
	return total
}

// executeDestroyAllArtifactsManaValueXOrLess implements the
// destroy_all_artifacts_mana_value_x_or_less primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-destroy-all-artifacts-mana-value-x-or-less-68cd40afab,
// Hammer Mage class): "Destroy all artifacts with mana value X or less."
// Follows executeSearchLibraryXAmountToHand's convention of reading the
// already-resolved X value out of effectValue["amount"] (folded in by the
// dynamic_amount.go dispatch chokepoints from whatever X was paid), so it
// slots into the same EffectAtom{Value: map[string]interface{}{"amount":
// ...}} calling convention as every other X-amount effect. Artifacts are
// matched against Card.Types the same way converter.go populates it from
// CardDefinition.Types ("Artifact" as a literal type-line word), then
// destroyed one at a time via DestroyTarget — the same single-target
// helper executeDestroyLowestManaValueNonlandPermanentEffect above uses,
// since this file has no bulk-destroy helper. Matches are collected into
// a slice before destroying so mutating the battlefield mid-loop can't
// skip a candidate.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyAllArtifactsManaValueXOrLess(effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	x := 0
	switch v := effectValue["amount"].(type) {
	case int:
		x = v
	case float64:
		x = int(v)
	}

	var toDestroy []*Card
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil {
			continue
		}
		isArtifact := false
		for _, t := range c.Types {
			if t == "Artifact" {
				isArtifact = true
				break
			}
		}
		if !isArtifact {
			continue
		}
		if c.ManaCost.CMC() <= x {
			toDestroy = append(toDestroy, c)
		}
	}

	for _, c := range toDestroy {
		if err := DestroyTarget(Target{IsCard: true, CardID: c.ID}, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeExileTemporaryReturnEffect implements the exile_temporary_return
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-exile-temporary-return-c20b682856,
// Explosive Getaway class): "Exile up to one target artifact or creature.
// Return it to the battlefield under its owner's control at the beginning
// of the next end step." Unlike DestroyTarget above (permanent removal to
// the graveyard) or a plain bounce (to hand), this removes the target from
// the battlefield and registers a one-shot EventEndStep subscriber closure
// that puts it back under its OWNER's control (not necessarily whoever
// controlled the exile effect) the very next time an end step begins — the
// delayed triggered ability Rule 603.7 describes. The delayed trigger is
// implemented as a self-contained, self-guarding closure (a local
// "returned" flag makes every firing after the first a no-op) rather than
// a new persistent GameState field, since it only ever needs to fire once
// per exiled object. "Up to one target" is represented upstream by an
// empty/absent targets slice, so calling this with no targets is a no-op.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileTemporaryReturnEffect(targets []Target) error {
	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		var card *Card
		for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
			if c != nil && c.ID == target.CardID {
				card = c
				break
			}
		}
		if card == nil {
			continue
		}
		gs.Battlefield.RemoveCard(card.ID)
		owner := card.Owner
		returned := false
		gs.EventBus.Subscribe(EventEndStep, func(ev *Event) {
			if returned {
				return
			}
			returned = true
			card.Controller = owner
			gs.Battlefield.AddCard(card)
		})
	}
	return nil
}

// QueueDestroyAtNextEndStep implements the destroy_at_next_end_step
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-destroy-at-next-end-step-286763de06, Old
// Hob, Alleycat Blues class): "Destroy it at the beginning of the next end
// step." Unlike an unconditional immediate destroy (DestroyTarget), this
// only records cardID onto the eotDestroyIDs queue — the actual destroy is
// deferred until DestroyQueuedAtNextEndStep drains the queue at the
// beginning of the next end step, mirroring the eotSacrificeIDs-style
// queues Blitz (game/blitz.go) and Encore (game/encore.go) populate for
// their own end-step-deferred actions.
func (gs *GameState) QueueDestroyAtNextEndStep(cardID string) {
	if cardID == "" {
		return
	}
	gs.eotDestroyIDs = append(gs.eotDestroyIDs, cardID)
}

// DestroyQueuedAtNextEndStep destroys every permanent queued by
// QueueDestroyAtNextEndStep. Mirrors sacrificeBlitzedAtEOT (game/blitz.go)
// — same queue-then-drain-at-end-step shape, meant to be called from the
// beginning of the end step. A queued permanent already gone from the
// battlefield (destroyed some other way, bounced, exiled, etc. before the
// end step arrived) is silently skipped, same as sacrificeBlitzedAtEOT's
// own GetCard-err check.
func (gs *GameState) DestroyQueuedAtNextEndStep() {
	if len(gs.eotDestroyIDs) == 0 {
		return
	}
	ids := gs.eotDestroyIDs
	gs.eotDestroyIDs = nil
	for _, id := range ids {
		if _, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(id); err != nil {
			continue
		}
		_ = DestroyTarget(Target{IsCard: true, CardID: id}, gs)
	}
}

// executeExileReturnNextEndStepEffect implements the
// exile_return_next_end_step primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-return-next-end-step-73c9c565f1, Skybind class): "Exile
// target permanent. Return that card to the battlefield under its owner's
// control at the beginning of the next end step." Unlike
// executeExileTemporaryReturnEffect above (an "up to one target artifact or
// creature" list, empty-slice no-op convention), this takes a single
// REQUIRED target of any permanent type — any filter restriction (e.g.
// Skybind's "nonenchantment") is enforced upstream at target selection, not
// here — so it's implemented as a thin single-target wrapper around the
// exact same proven delayed-return mechanics (exile now, register a
// one-shot EventEndStep subscriber that puts the exact same card back under
// its owner's control the next time an end step begins).
func (gs *GameState) executeExileReturnNextEndStepEffect(target Target) error {
	if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
		return nil
	}
	return gs.executeExileTemporaryReturnEffect([]Target{target})
}

// executeDurableSelfExileEffect implements the durable_self_exile primitive
// (ticket engine.auto-durable-self-exile-dd89277b18, Glorious Protector
// class): "exile any number of <filter> permanents you control until this
// creature leaves the battlefield." Unlike executeExileTemporaryReturnEffect
// above (a fixed next-end-step delayed return) or the plain exile_target
// case (one-way, no return clause), this is a DURABLE, self-referential
// duration: the exiled permanents return to the battlefield automatically
// when the SOURCE permanent leaves the battlefield. The engine already has
// exactly this return machinery — Card.StashedExileIDs (card.go) plus
// GameState.returnStashedExiledCards, which UnregisterCardAbilities calls at
// the moment a card leaves the battlefield (gamestate.go) — so this
// executor's only job is the mass, filter-scoped battlefield-sourced exile
// half: it scans the battlefield for every permanent controlled by
// controller that matches the filter (matchesPermanentFilter, the same
// type-bucket matcher the exile_all_from_zone case uses; an empty filter
// means "any permanent") and is NOT itself the source, exiles each into its
// owner's exile zone, and records each ID on source.StashedExileIDs so the
// existing leave-the-battlefield hook returns them all. Matches are
// collected into a slice before moving so removing cards mid-scan can't
// skip a candidate. No-op if source is nil or no permanents match.
func (gs *GameState) executeDurableSelfExileEffect(source *Card, controller int, filter string) error {
	if source == nil {
		return nil
	}
	var victims []*Card
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || c.ID == source.ID || c.Controller != controller {
			continue
		}
		if filter != "" && !matchesPermanentFilter(c, filter) {
			continue
		}
		victims = append(victims, c)
	}
	for _, victim := range victims {
		gs.Battlefield.RemoveCard(victim.ID)
		gs.UnregisterCardAbilities(victim)
		if victim.Owner >= 0 && victim.Owner < len(gs.Players) {
			gs.Players[victim.Owner].Exile.AddCard(victim)
		}
		source.StashedExileIDs = append(source.StashedExileIDs, victim.ID)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeSelfExileFromGraveyardWithDependentTrigger implements the atomic
// "exile ~ from your graveyard. When you do, <effect>" shape: it moves
// source out of controller's graveyard into its owner's exile zone and,
// only if that exile actually occurs, immediately runs the bound dependent
// effect as part of the same resolution. If source is not currently in the
// graveyard, neither the exile nor the dependent effect happen — the two
// are one coupled primitive, not two independently-triggered steps.
func (gs *GameState) executeSelfExileFromGraveyardWithDependentTrigger(source *Card, controller int, dependent func(gs *GameState, source *Card, controller int) error) (bool, error) {
	if source == nil || controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return false, nil
	}
	var found *Card
	for _, c := range gs.Players[controller].Graveyard.GetAll() {
		if c != nil && c.ID == source.ID {
			found = c
			break
		}
	}
	if found == nil {
		return false, nil
	}
	gs.Players[controller].Graveyard.RemoveCard(found.ID)
	owner := found.Owner
	if owner < 0 || owner >= len(gs.Players) {
		owner = controller
	}
	gs.Players[owner].Exile.AddCard(found)
	if dependent == nil {
		return true, nil
	}
	return true, dependent(gs, found, controller)
}

// executeSacrificeSelfReturnExiled implements the return_exiled_cards_with_source
// primitive (Promise of Tomorrow class: "sacrifice this ~ and return all
// cards exiled with it to the battlefield under your control"). Unlike the
// shared returnStashedExiledCards helper (used by other stash consumers such
// as the exile-until-monarch-change family, which return under a card's
// OWNER's control per its own wording), this effect's cards return under the
// SOURCE's controller specifically, so the controller is captured before the
// source leaves the battlefield and applied explicitly rather than relying
// on whatever Controller value each card happened to carry into exile.
func (gs *GameState) executeSacrificeSelfReturnExiled(source *Card, controller int) error {
	if source == nil {
		return nil
	}
	ids := append([]string(nil), source.StashedExileIDs...)
	returnController := source.Controller
	gs.sacrificeCard(source, controller)
	for _, id := range ids {
		for _, p := range gs.Players {
			if c, err := p.Exile.RemoveCard(id); err == nil {
				c.Controller = returnController
				c.SummoningSick = true
				gs.Battlefield.AddCard(c)
				gs.RegisterCardAbilities(c)
				break
			}
		}
	}
	source.StashedExileIDs = nil
	return nil
}

// linkedTokenExileRegistry maps a source permanent's ID to the IDs of
// tokens created by an EARLIER effect of that permanent's own ability,
// pending exile when the source leaves the battlefield (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-linked-tokens-on-leaves-battlefield-1bf674ba89,
// Mysterio, Master of Illusion class: "Exile those tokens when Mysterio
// leaves the battlefield."). Keyed by source.ID rather than a Card field
// since the referent here is exactly the token set an earlier effect IN
// THE SAME ABILITY produced ("those tokens") -- not a battlefield filter
// query (that shape is the "all"-filter mass-exile case elsewhere in this
// file) -- and it runs the opposite direction from
// executeDurableSelfExileEffect's StashedExileIDs/return machinery just
// above (exile now, return on leave): here tokens sit on the battlefield
// until the source leaves, at which point they are exiled.
var linkedTokenExileRegistry = map[string][]string{}

// executeCreateLinkedExileTokens creates spec.Count copies of a single
// creature token shape under controller's control (construction mirrors
// executeCreateTokenFullDefinitionEffect) and records their IDs against
// source.ID in linkedTokenExileRegistry, so a later
// executeExileLinkedTokensOnLeave call for the same source exiles exactly
// this set rather than re-querying the battlefield by filter. No-op
// (nil, nil) if source or gs.Battlefield is nil, or spec.Count <= 0.
func (gs *GameState) executeCreateLinkedExileTokens(source *Card, controller int, spec TokenFullDefinitionSpec) ([]*Card, error) {
	if source == nil || gs.Battlefield == nil || spec.Count <= 0 || spec.Name == "" {
		return nil, nil
	}
	tokens := make([]*Card, 0, spec.Count)
	for i := 0; i < spec.Count; i++ {
		token := NewCreature(spec.Name, ManaCost{}, spec.Power, spec.Toughness, 0)
		token.SubTypes = []string{spec.SubType}
		token.Colors = []Color{spec.Color}
		if len(spec.Keywords) > 0 {
			token.Keywords = append(token.Keywords, spec.Keywords...)
		}
		token.Owner = controller
		token.Controller = controller
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(token)
		tokens = append(tokens, token)
	}
	ids := make([]string, len(tokens))
	for i, t := range tokens {
		ids[i] = t.ID
	}
	linkedTokenExileRegistry[source.ID] = append(linkedTokenExileRegistry[source.ID], ids...)
	return tokens, nil
}

// executeExileLinkedTokensOnLeave implements the
// exile_linked_tokens_on_leaves_battlefield primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-linked-tokens-on-leaves-battlefield-1bf674ba89,
// Mysterio, Master of Illusion class). Unlike
// executeDurableSelfExileEffect above (a battlefield-filter scan that
// exiles NOW and returns later), the recipient set here was fixed at
// token-creation time by executeCreateLinkedExileTokens -- exactly the
// tokens a prior effect of this same ability produced -- so a permanent
// that merely matches the tokens' type/subtype but wasn't one of the
// tokens THIS source created is never touched. Intended to be called once
// source has already left the battlefield: each linked token still on the
// battlefield is moved to its owner's exile zone (mirroring
// executeDurableSelfExileEffect's move-to-exile shape), and the registry
// entry for source.ID is cleared either way. No-op if source is nil or
// has no linked tokens recorded.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileLinkedTokensOnLeave(source *Card) error {
	if source == nil {
		return nil
	}
	ids := linkedTokenExileRegistry[source.ID]
	delete(linkedTokenExileRegistry, source.ID)
	for _, id := range ids {
		victim, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(id)
		if err != nil || victim == nil {
			continue
		}
		gs.Battlefield.RemoveCard(victim.ID)
		gs.UnregisterCardAbilities(victim)
		if victim.Owner >= 0 && victim.Owner < len(gs.Players) {
			gs.Players[victim.Owner].Exile.AddCard(victim)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeMenaceCounterOnControlledCreature implements the
// menace_counter_on_controlled_creature primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-menace-counter-on-controlled-creature-372766fb5b, Blood
// Curdle class): "Put a menace counter on a creature you control," the
// second clause of "Destroy target creature. Put a menace counter on a
// creature you control." The destroy clause resolves through the
// existing single-target destroy primitive as its own preceding effect
// atom in the spell's sequence — this atom only ever places the counter.
// The clause names no target (unlike the TargetCreatureYouCtl-style
// secondary targets elsewhere in this file), so there is no player-choice
// input to consume here; mirroring bolster's self-selection below, the
// first creature found controlled by controller is chosen, with no
// tiebreak rule since the source text gives none. Only ever considers
// Battlefield creatures, never a player, so a sequence naming a player
// target for this clause stays unmapped by construction. No-op if
// controller controls no creatures.
func (gs *GameState) executeMenaceCounterOnControlledCreature(controller int) error {
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c != nil && c.Controller == controller && c.IsCreature() {
			gs.AddCounters(c, CounterType("menace"), 1)
			return nil
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeTapAnyNumberUntappedYouControlEffect implements the
// tap_any_number_untapped_you_control primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-tap-any-number-untapped-you-control-68f1518802, Harmony of
// Nature class): "Tap any number of untapped creatures you control." The
// engine has no interactive per-permanent selection UI (the same
// auto-select convention HeritageDruid/topplegeistOpponentCreatureToTap
// use elsewhere), and here tapping is never harmful on its own — Harmony
// of Nature immediately follows with "You gain 4 life for each creature
// tapped this way," so the maximal, all-beneficial choice is to tap every
// untapped creature controller controls. Returns the count tapped so a
// caller can scale a following "for each creature tapped this way" effect;
// already-tapped creatures and creatures controlled by anyone else are
// left untouched.
func (gs *GameState) executeTapAnyNumberUntappedYouControlEffect(controller int) (int, error) {
	tapped := 0
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || c.Controller != controller || !c.IsCreature() || c.Tapped {
			continue
		}
		c.Tapped = true
		tapped++
	}
	return tapped, nil
}

// executeTapChooseUntappedCreaturesYouControl implements the
// tap_choose_untapped_creatures_you_control primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-tap-choose-untapped-creatures-you-control-34de4bacb5, Tidal
// Terror class): "tap two other untapped creatures you control" as a
// resolution-time, NON-targeted choice — distinct from the Target-based
// executeTapEffect path ("tap target creature") and from the tap_other
// activation-cost / executeSacrificeFiltered PendingChoice shapes, which
// both ask for an announce-time or a real interactive selection. The engine
// has no interactive per-permanent selection UI for this class of effect
// (the same auto-select convention executeTapAnyNumberUntappedYouControlEffect
// just above uses), so candidates are taken in battlefield order. Per Rule
// 601.2c, "choose exactly N" is only legal when at least N candidates
// exist — if fewer than n untapped creatures the controller controls
// (excluding source) are available, the whole choice fails and nothing is
// tapped, rather than tapping a partial, illegal-sized set. Completion is
// recorded under seqTally["tapped_creature"] (the same "tapped_"+filter key
// executeTapAllEffect's tappedThisWay tally uses), a no-op outside a
// sequence walk, so a following "if you do" atom in the same sequence can
// gate on whether the full N-choice actually happened.
func (gs *GameState) executeTapChooseUntappedCreaturesYouControl(controller int, source *Card, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	n := getIntValue(effectValue, "n", 2)
	if n <= 0 {
		return nil
	}
	var candidates []*Card
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || c.Controller != controller || !c.IsCreature() || c.Tapped {
			continue
		}
		if source != nil && c.ID == source.ID {
			continue
		}
		candidates = append(candidates, c)
	}
	if len(candidates) < n {
		gs.noteSeqTally("tapped_creature", 0)
		return nil
	}
	for i := 0; i < n; i++ {
		card := candidates[i]
		card.Tapped = true
		if gs.EventBus != nil {
			gs.ProcessTriggeredAbilities(NewEvent(EventTapped).WithSource(card).WithPlayer(card.Controller))
			gs.ProcessTriggeredAbilities(NewEvent(EventBecomesTapped).WithSource(card).WithPlayer(card.Controller))
		}
	}
	gs.noteSeqTally("tapped_creature", n)
	return nil
}

// executeTapWithStunCounterEffect implements the tap_with_stun_counter
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-tap-with-stun-counter-1c66e799e6,
// Frostfist Strider class): "Tap target creature an opponent controls and
// put a stun counter on it." One sentence naming ONE referent but
// performing TWO effects — tapping the permanent and placing a single
// stun counter on it (Rule 701.45a: if a permanent with a stun counter
// would become untapped, remove one stun counter instead) — executed
// against the same target rather than requiring two separate effect
// atoms. Mirrors executeMenaceCounterOnControlledCreature just above for
// the AddCounters call, and the gain_control_referent_eot case's
// Battlefield.GetCard lookup for resolving the target card. Tapping sets
// Card.Tapped directly (the same field executeUntapAllEffect flips via
// Untap()) rather than dispatching an event — no card in this primitive's
// class reacts to becoming tapped, only to the stun counter's later
// untap-skip. Targets that aren't cards, or that no longer resolve on the
// battlefield, are silently skipped (mirrors
// executeExileTemporaryReturnEffect's target loop).
func (gs *GameState) executeTapWithStunCounterEffect(targets []Target, effectValueArgs ...map[string]interface{}) error {
	var effectValue map[string]interface{}
	if len(effectValueArgs) > 0 {
		effectValue = effectValueArgs[0]
	}
	// put_stun_counters primitive (ticket
	// engine.auto-put-stun-counters-5fbf4744fe, Tranquilize class): "Tap
	// target creature an opponent controls and put three stun counters on
	// it." Same tap+stun-on-same-referent shape as Frostfist Strider, but
	// with a variable count instead of a fixed one, so the amount is read
	// from effectValue["amount"] and defaults to 1 to preserve the
	// existing single-stun-counter callers unchanged.
	amount := 1
	switch v := effectValue["amount"].(type) {
	case int:
		if v > 0 {
			amount = v
		}
	case float64:
		if v > 0 {
			amount = int(v)
		}
	}
	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		card.Tapped = true
		gs.AddCounters(card, CounterType("stun"), amount)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeStunCounterApplyEffect implements the stun_counter_apply primitive
// (ticket engine.auto-stun-counter-apply-c12fb588c1, Stall Out class): "Tap
// target creature or Vehicle, then put three stun counters on it." Same
// tap-then-counter shape as executeTapWithStunCounterEffect just above, but
// the number of stun counters is a caller-specified amount rather than a
// fixed 1 (Rule 701.45a's untap-replacement — a permanent with a stun
// counter removes one instead of untapping — is already enforced by the
// engine's untap step wherever counters of this type are found, so this
// primitive only needs to place them). amount<=0 falls back to 1, matching
// the always-at-least-one-counter reading of "put N stun counters" for any
// N a caller might legitimately pass. Targets that aren't cards, or that no
// longer resolve on the battlefield, are silently skipped (mirrors
// executeTapWithStunCounterEffect's target-loop convention).
func (gs *GameState) executeStunCounterApplyEffect(targets []Target, amount int) error {
	if amount <= 0 {
		amount = 1
	}
	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		card.Tapped = true
		gs.AddCounters(card, CounterType("stun"), amount)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeTapVariableAmountXEffect implements the tap_variable_amount_x
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-tap-variable-amount-x-dce68dda2c, Glimpse
// the Sun God class): "Tap X target creatures." Unlike
// executeTapWithStunCounterEffect just above (one named referent, tapped
// unconditionally) or a fixed/up-to-N target count enforced purely by
// upstream target selection, the printed amount here is the VARIABLE X a
// player paid — already resolved to a plain int in effectValue["amount"] by
// the two dynamic_amount.go dispatch chokepoints (ExecuteAbilityEffect /
// ApplySpellEffectWithRuntime), mirroring how executeSearchLibraryXAmountToHand
// (effects.go) reads its own X-derived "amount". Since upstream target
// selection may hand back more targets than X, this caps tapping at
// effectValue["amount"] rather than trusting len(targets) — tapping only
// the first `amount` resolvable targets in the order given and leaving any
// remainder untouched, so "Tap X target creatures" never taps more than X
// regardless of how many targets were gathered. Targets that aren't cards,
// or that no longer resolve on the battlefield, are silently skipped
// (mirrors executeTapWithStunCounterEffect's target-loop convention) and a
// skip does not consume one of the amount slots reserved for
// still-resolvable targets later in the slice. amount<=0 (e.g. X paid as
// {0}, Rule 601.2b) is a no-op. effectValue["filter"], when present,
// additionally restricts which resolved targets count toward X (Gridlock
// class, ticket engine.auto-tap-variable-targets-c195710275: "Tap X target
// nonland permanents") — "nonland_permanent"/"nonland" excludes lands via
// Card.IsLand() the same way executeDestroyLowestManaValueNonlandPermanentEffect
// does, and any other non-empty value defers to matchesPermanentFilter, the
// same generic type-bucket matcher executeTapAllEffect's filter uses. An
// absent/empty filter preserves the original unrestricted behavior.
func (gs *GameState) executeTapVariableAmountXEffect(targets []Target, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	amount := 0
	switch v := effectValue["amount"].(type) {
	case int:
		amount = v
	case float64:
		amount = int(v)
	}
	if amount <= 0 {
		return nil
	}
	filter := getStringValue(effectValue, "filter", "")
	tapped := 0
	for _, target := range targets {
		if tapped >= amount {
			break
		}
		if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		switch filter {
		case "":
		case "nonland_permanent", "nonland":
			if card.IsLand() {
				continue
			}
		default:
			if !matchesPermanentFilter(card, filter) {
				continue
			}
		}
		card.Tapped = true
		tapped++
	}
	return nil
}

// executeUntapUpToDynamicCapEffect implements the
// untap_with_event_derived_cap primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-untap-with-event-derived-cap-9363cce197, Shriekwood Devourer
// class): "Whenever you attack with one or more creatures, untap up to X
// lands, where X is the greatest power among those creatures." Unlike
// executeUntapAllEffect's blanket unconditional untap (which deliberately
// no-ops on "up_to"/"amount" for lack of a real partial-N choice flow),
// this is exactly that flow for the "cap comes from the trigger, not a
// literal" shape: X is folded into effectValue["amount"] upstream by the
// max_stat_of_group dynamic-amount spec's "attacking" qualifier
// (dynamic_amount.go), then this caps untapping at that amount the same
// way executeTapVariableAmountXEffect caps tapping at a paid {X} — walking
// the pre-selected candidate targets in order and stopping once `amount`
// of them have actually been untapped. Calls Untap() and fires
// EventUntapped per card (matching executeUntapAllEffect's per-card event
// convention) instead of setting Tapped directly. Already-untapped
// candidates are skipped without consuming a slot (untapping nothing isn't
// a real choice), and unresolvable targets or targets failing `filter`
// (default "land", matching the Shriekwood Devourer class) are skipped
// too. amount<=0 is a no-op (a legal "up to 0" choice, Rule 601.2b analog).
func (gs *GameState) executeUntapUpToDynamicCapEffect(targets []Target, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	amount := 0
	switch v := effectValue["amount"].(type) {
	case int:
		amount = v
	case float64:
		amount = int(v)
	}
	if amount <= 0 {
		return nil
	}
	filter := getStringValue(effectValue, "filter", "land")
	untapped := 0
	for _, target := range targets {
		if untapped >= amount {
			break
		}
		if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		if filter != "" && !matchesPermanentFilter(card, filter) {
			continue
		}
		if !card.Tapped {
			continue
		}
		card.Untap()
		if gs.EventBus != nil {
			gs.ProcessTriggeredAbilities(NewEvent(EventUntapped).WithSource(card).WithPlayer(card.Controller))
		}
		untapped++
	}
	return nil
}

// executeGrantDynamicPowerEqualToHandSizeEOTEffect implements the
// grant_dynamic_power_eot primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-grant-dynamic-power-eot-f0aada431d, Ms. Marvel, Kamala Khan
// class): "Until end of turn, ~ gains '~'s base power is equal to the
// number of cards in your hand.'" Unlike a fixed EOT pump (a static
// PowerMod/BasePower delta) or a granted static keyword, this reads the
// controller's CURRENT hand size at the moment it runs and overwrites
// source.BasePower with it directly — mirroring
// executeTemporaryCharacteristicOverwriteEOTEffect's plain BasePower-
// mutation idiom rather than layering an additive ContinuousEffect.
// Callers re-invoke this whenever the hand size may have changed (e.g. on
// re-trigger later the same turn), so the base power stays current for
// the rest of the turn. No-op if source is nil or controller is out of
// range.
func (gs *GameState) executeGrantDynamicPowerEqualToHandSizeEOTEffect(source *Card, controller int) error {
	if source == nil || controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	handSize := gs.Players[controller].Hand.Count()
	source.BasePower = &handSize
	return nil
}

// PowerToughnessUnequal implements the power_toughness_inequality_filter
// permanent-filter bucket primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-power-toughness-inequality-filter-f31bdd4048, Gilt-Leaf
// Winnower class): "destroy target ... creature whose power and toughness
// aren't equal." Matches a permanent only when its CURRENT power differs
// from its CURRENT toughness. Read straight off Card.BasePower/
// BaseToughness rather than any printed/characteristic-defining snapshot —
// every P/T modifier this engine applies (CdaPTSpec recomputes, EOT
// pump/shrink effects) writes directly into these two fields, so they
// already reflect continuous effects at read time. Symmetric +1/+1 or
// -1/-1 counters need no special handling here: they add the same delta to
// both sides, so they can never flip an equal P/T into an unequal one or
// vice versa — only an asymmetric modifier (e.g. a 4/4 pumped to 5/3) can.
// Non-creatures (nil BasePower/BaseToughness) never match.
func PowerToughnessUnequal(c *Card) bool {
	if c == nil || c.BasePower == nil || c.BaseToughness == nil {
		return false
	}
	return *c.BasePower != *c.BaseToughness
}

// executeDestroyPowerToughnessUnequalTargetEffect implements the destroy
// verb consuming the power_toughness_inequality_filter bucket above (Rule
// 608.2b: an illegal target at resolution is skipped, not destroyed) —
// e.g. Gilt-Leaf Winnower's "destroy target non-Elf creature whose power
// and toughness aren't equal." A target chosen when legal can become
// illegal by resolution (pumped/shrunk back to equal P/T) or vice versa;
// this re-checks PowerToughnessUnequal against LIVE state immediately
// before destroying, mirroring executeExileTemporaryReturnEffect's
// target-loop skip convention above. Targets that no longer resolve to a
// battlefield permanent are likewise silently skipped.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyPowerToughnessUnequalTargetEffect(targets []Target) error {
	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		if !PowerToughnessUnequal(card) {
			continue
		}
		if err := DestroyTarget(Target{IsCard: true, CardID: card.ID}, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeDestroyExcludingSubtypesEffect implements the
// destroy_target_excluding_subtypes primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-single-target-destroy-creature-excluding-subtype-b7eec0f39d,
// Power Word Kill class): "Destroy target non-Angel, non-Demon, non-Devil,
// non-Dragon creature." Mirrors
// executeDestroyPowerToughnessUnequalTargetEffect's resolution-time
// re-check convention just above (Rule 608.2b): a target chosen as legal
// at cast time can gain an excluded subtype before resolution (or vice
// versa), so SubTypes is re-checked against the LIVE battlefield card
// immediately before destroying, rather than trusting the choice made at
// targeting time. Any overlap with excludeSubtypes makes the target
// illegal and it is skipped, not destroyed — the exclusion set is honored
// exactly, never widened or narrowed.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyExcludingSubtypesEffect(targets []Target, excludeSubtypes []string) error {
	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		if cardHasAnySubtype(card, excludeSubtypes) {
			continue
		}
		if err := DestroyTarget(Target{IsCard: true, CardID: card.ID}, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// cardHasAnySubtype reports whether card.SubTypes contains any of the
// given subtypes (exact string match, mirroring the Oracle text's own
// capitalized subtype names as emitted by the DSL/converter).
func cardHasAnySubtype(card *Card, subtypes []string) bool {
	for _, st := range subtypes {
		for _, cst := range card.SubTypes {
			if cst == st {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

// subtypesFromEffectValue normalizes an effect_value subtype list (parsed
// from JSON, so a []interface{} of strings) into a []string. Returns nil
// for any other shape or an empty/absent value, mirroring
// getStringValue's zero-value-on-mismatch convention used elsewhere in
// this file.
func subtypesFromEffectValue(raw interface{}) []string {
	items, ok := raw.([]interface{})
	if !ok {
		return nil
	}
	out := make([]string, 0, len(items))
	for _, item := range items {
		if s, ok := item.(string); ok && s != "" {
			out = append(out, s)
		}
	}
	return out
}

// executeDestroyTargetAndSameNameOthers implements the
// destroy_target_and_same_name_others primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-destroy-target-and-same-name-others-20712b782c, Echoing Calm
// class): "Destroy target enchantment and all other enchantments with the
// same name as that enchantment." Unlike a static all-of-type mass destroy
// (no target reference) or a plain single-target destroy (no companion mass
// destroy), this is a referent-driven mass filter keyed off the JUST-TARGETED
// permanent's own name: the target's name is captured, every OTHER
// battlefield permanent sharing that exact name is collected, the target
// itself is destroyed via the existing DestroyTarget primitive, and then
// each collected namesake is destroyed the same way. The namesake set is
// captured before the target is destroyed so removing it from the
// battlefield can't affect the name comparison. Namesake destroy errors are
// swallowed (best-effort, mirrors state-based-action cleanup possibly having
// already removed a permanent) — only the initial targeted destroy's error
// propagates. No-op if the target isn't a card or isn't found.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyTargetAndSameNameOthers(target Target) error {
	if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
		return nil
	}
	var named *Card
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c != nil && c.ID == target.CardID {
			named = c
			break
		}
	}
	if named == nil {
		return nil
	}
	name := named.Name
	var otherIDs []string
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c != nil && c.ID != target.CardID && c.Name == name {
			otherIDs = append(otherIDs, c.ID)
		}
	}
	if err := DestroyTarget(target, gs); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	for _, id := range otherIDs {
		_ = DestroyTarget(Target{IsCard: true, CardID: id}, gs)
	}
	return nil
}

// resolveSameNameAsTargetFilter implements the same_name_as_target_filter
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-same-name-as-target-filter-ba6327bdcd,
// Echoing Courage class): "Target creature and all other creatures with
// the same name as that creature get +2/+2 until end of turn." The pump
// executor only ever applies its bonus to whatever is in the targets
// slice it's handed, so this primitive doesn't touch power/toughness at
// all — it only EXPANDS targets, before dispatch, at the SAME
// before-the-switch chokepoint as resolvePumpAttachedCreatureNoTarget
// just above. Gated by EffectValue["same_name_as_target"] (bool) so a
// plain "target creature" pump with no such flag is left completely
// untouched (negative example: it must not be broadened). For each
// already-targeted card, every OTHER battlefield creature sharing its
// Name is appended as an implicit extra target — never asked for or
// chosen individually, matching the required "without requiring those
// additional creatures to be individually targeted." Already-targeted
// IDs are tracked in seen so nothing is ever pumped twice in one
// resolution, and only creatures (IsCreature()) are considered, mirroring
// executeMenaceCounterOnControlledCreature's plain Battlefield.GetAll()
// scan just above.
// executeReturnTargetAndSameNameOthersFromGraveyard implements the
// return_from_graveyard_copy_name primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-from-graveyard-copy-name-ad36e1f317, Rat King,
// Verminister class): "Return target creature card and all other cards
// with the same name as that card from your graveyard to the battlefield
// tapped." Mirrors executeDestroyTargetAndSameNameOthers's target-name-
// capture-then-mass-match idiom just above (Echoing Calm class), but keyed
// off the CONTROLLER's own graveyard (Rule 400.4 — graveyard is a
// per-player zone) rather than the shared battlefield, and moves cards TO
// the battlefield (RemoveCard/AddCard, executeReturnFromGraveyardManaValueX's
// idiom) instead of destroying them. The target's own name is captured
// before any removal so a shrinking graveyard mid-loop can't affect the
// name comparison, and a same-named card sitting in a DIFFERENT player's
// graveyard is never touched. Every returned card enters under the
// CONTROLLER's control, tapped per the optional tapped flag.
func (gs *GameState) executeReturnTargetAndSameNameOthersFromGraveyard(controller int, target Target, tapped bool) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
		return nil
	}
	graveyard := gs.Players[controller].Graveyard
	var named *Card
	for _, c := range graveyard.GetAll() {
		if c != nil && c.ID == target.CardID {
			named = c
			break
		}
	}
	if named == nil {
		return nil
	}
	name := named.Name
	cards := []*Card{named}
	for _, c := range graveyard.GetAll() {
		if c != nil && c.ID != target.CardID && c.Name == name {
			cards = append(cards, c)
		}
	}
	for _, c := range cards {
		if _, err := graveyard.RemoveCard(c.ID); err != nil {
			continue
		}
		c.Controller = controller
		c.Tapped = tapped
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(c)
	}
	return nil
}

func (gs *GameState) resolveSameNameAsTargetFilter(effect string, effectValue map[string]interface{}, targets []Target) []Target {
	if subtype, ok := effectValue["target_creature_subtype"].(string); ok && subtype != "" && (effect == "pump" || effect == "pump_until_eot") {
		targets = gs.filterTargetsByCreatureSubtype(targets, subtype)
	}
	if effect != "pump" {
		return targets
	}
	applyTypeRemoval := func(ts []Target) []Target {
		if losesTypes, _ := effectValue["loses_all_creature_types"].(bool); losesTypes {
			gs.stripCreatureTypesUntilEOT(ts)
		}
		return ts
	}
	if globalFilter, ok := effectValue["global_filter"].(map[string]interface{}); ok {
		return applyTypeRemoval(gs.resolvePumpGlobalFilterTargets(globalFilter))
	}
	if all, _ := effectValue["all"].(bool); all {
		return applyTypeRemoval(gs.resolvePumpAllFilterTargets(effectValue))
	}
	same, _ := effectValue["same_name_as_target"].(bool)
	if !same {
		return applyTypeRemoval(targets)
	}
	seen := map[string]bool{}
	for _, t := range targets {
		if t.IsCard {
			seen[t.CardID] = true
		}
	}
	var names []string
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
			if c != nil && c.ID == t.CardID {
				names = append(names, c.Name)
				break
			}
		}
	}
	if len(names) == 0 {
		return applyTypeRemoval(targets)
	}
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || !c.IsCreature() || seen[c.ID] {
			continue
		}
		for _, name := range names {
			if c.Name == name {
				targets = append(targets, Target{IsCard: true, CardID: c.ID})
				seen[c.ID] = true
				break
			}
		}
	}
	return applyTypeRemoval(targets)
}

func (gs *GameState) stripCreatureTypesUntilEOT(targets []Target) {
	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil || !card.IsCreature() {
			continue
		}
		card.SubTypes = nil
	}
}

// filterTargetsByCreatureSubtype implements the target_filter_creature_subtype
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-target-filter-creature-subtype-eff5ac9036,
// Snarling Undorak class): "Target Beast creature gets +1/+1 until end of
// turn." Narrows an already-chosen targets slice, at the same
// before-the-switch chokepoint as resolveSameNameAsTargetFilter just above,
// to only the targets that are creatures carrying the given subtype
// (Card.SubTypes, Rule 201.4 exact-name match, mirroring
// resolvePumpGlobalFilterTargets's exclude_subtype check one primitive
// below). A target that fails the check is simply dropped rather than
// causing an error, matching Rule 601.2c/608.2b's "illegal target" handling
// used elsewhere in this file (e.g.
// executeDestroyTargetIfManaValueEqualsUnspentMana above).
func (gs *GameState) filterTargetsByCreatureSubtype(targets []Target, subtype string) []Target {
	var result []Target
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil || !card.IsCreature() {
			continue
		}
		for _, st := range card.SubTypes {
			if st == subtype {
				result = append(result, t)
				break
			}
		}
	}
	return result
}

// resolvePumpTargetOwnPower implements the pump_amount_equals_target_power
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-pump-amount-equals-target-power-444b5b714c,
// Nantuko Mentor class: "Target creature gets +X/+X until end of turn, where
// X is that creature's power."). X is read directly off the SAME creature
// the pump targets, at resolution time (Rule 608.2g — a value referencing a
// target's characteristic is locked in only when the ability resolves,
// using the target's then-current power), via GetPowerWithEffects so any
// earlier continuous pump or counter already on the target is included.
// Keyed by EffectValue["power_from"]=="target_own_power" (closed, single
// value) so a fixed numeric pump amount or a pump_x_source-derived count
// (e.g. "number of Elves you control") is never routed through this fold.
func (gs *GameState) resolvePumpTargetOwnPower(effectValue map[string]interface{}, targets []Target) map[string]interface{} {
	if effectValue == nil {
		return effectValue
	}
	from, _ := effectValue["power_from"].(string)
	if from != "target_own_power" {
		return effectValue
	}
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		p := card.GetPowerWithEffects(gs.EffectManager)
		effectValue["power"] = p
		effectValue["toughness"] = p
		break
	}
	return effectValue
}

// resolvePumpGlobalFilterTargets implements the pump_global_until_eot
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-pump-global-until-eot-c07706d1e2, Noxious
// Ghoul class): "all non-Zombie creatures get -1/-1 until end of turn."
// Unlike resolveSameNameAsTargetFilter above (which EXPANDS an existing
// target list), this REPLACES targets entirely with every battlefield
// creature matching the filter — Oracle text here names no target at all,
// it's a mass "all ... creatures" effect. Reuses the same before-the-switch
// chokepoint (called from resolveSameNameAsTargetFilter, gated by the
// "global_filter" key so a plain targeted pump — no such key — is left
// completely untouched, satisfying the "targeted pump effects" negative
// example) so the pump executor downstream still just applies its
// power/toughness delta to whatever targets slice it's handed, exactly as
// it does for a single-target pump — no new duration handling is needed
// since plain "pump" is already an until-end-of-turn effect in this
// engine, satisfying the "pump effects with different durations" negative
// example by construction (this primitive never touches duration). Only
// "exclude_subtype" is supported (closed vocabulary matching this
// ticket's scope): when set, any creature with that subtype
// (Card.SubTypes, Rule 201.4 exact-name match) is skipped; every other
// creature on the battlefield, regardless of controller, is included,
// matching Oracle's unqualified "all ... creatures" (no "you control"
// restriction).
func (gs *GameState) resolvePumpGlobalFilterTargets(filter map[string]interface{}) []Target {
	excludeSubtype, _ := filter["exclude_subtype"].(string)
	var targets []Target
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || !c.IsCreature() {
			continue
		}
		if excludeSubtype != "" {
			excluded := false
			for _, st := range c.SubTypes {
				if st == excludeSubtype {
					excluded = true
					break
				}
			}
			if excluded {
				continue
			}
		}
		targets = append(targets, Target{IsCard: true, CardID: c.ID})
	}
	return targets
}

// resolvePumpAllFilterTargets implements the pump_until_eot_no_target
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-pump-until-eot-no-target-cead48b9ac, Rolling
// Spoil class): "all creatures get -1/-1 until end of turn." Unlike
// resolvePumpGlobalFilterTargets above (gated by the nested "global_filter"
// map key), this reads the flat no-single-target filter shape named by this
// ticket — effectValue["all"]==true plus effectValue["category"] — and
// REPLACES targets with every matching battlefield permanent, so the pump
// executor downstream still just applies its power/toughness delta to
// whatever targets slice it's handed, exactly as for a single-target pump.
// Only "creature" is a recognized category (closed vocabulary matching this
// ticket's scope, Rolling Spoil's "all creatures"); any other or absent
// category value is left completely untouched by the caller (fails closed)
// rather than guessed at. A plain targeted pump (no "all" key at all) never
// reaches this function, satisfying the "targeted pump" negative example.
func (gs *GameState) resolvePumpAllFilterTargets(effectValue map[string]interface{}) []Target {
	category, _ := effectValue["category"].(string)
	if category != "creature" {
		return nil
	}
	var targets []Target
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || !c.IsCreature() {
			continue
		}
		targets = append(targets, Target{IsCard: true, CardID: c.ID})
	}
	return targets
}

// resolveAttackingSameDefenderAsSourceFilter implements the
// filter_attacking_same_defender_as_source primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-filter-attacking-same-defender-as-source-678c551aed, Kitesail
// Skirmisher class: "another target creature attacking the same player or
// planeswalker gains flying until end of turn."). Unlike
// resolveSameNameAsTargetFilter above, this doesn't EXPAND targets — it
// NARROWS an already-chosen targets slice, dropping any target that isn't
// attacking, that IS the source itself ("another"), or that is attacking a
// different defender than source is, BEFORE dispatch — same before-the-
// switch chokepoint. Gated by EffectValue["target_filter"] ==
// "attacking_same_defender_as_source" so an ordinary "another target
// attacking creature" grant with no such flag (negative example) is left
// completely untouched. "Same defender" compares AttackTarget (Rule
// 506.4c's planeswalker-attack pointer, card.go): nil on both sides means
// both attack the sole defending player in this two-player engine; a
// non-nil AttackTarget must match by ID on both sides.
func (gs *GameState) resolveAttackingSameDefenderAsSourceFilter(source *Card, effectValue map[string]interface{}, targets []Target) []Target {
	filter, _ := effectValue["target_filter"].(string)
	if filter != "attacking_same_defender_as_source" || source == nil {
		return targets
	}
	out := targets[:0]
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == source.ID {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil || !card.Attacking {
			continue
		}
		if source.AttackTarget != nil || card.AttackTarget != nil {
			if source.AttackTarget == nil || card.AttackTarget == nil || source.AttackTarget.ID != card.AttackTarget.ID {
				continue
			}
		}
		out = append(out, t)
	}
	return out
}

// resolveAttackingCreatureWithoutKeywordFilter implements the
// filter_attacking_creature_without_keyword primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-filter-attacking-creature-without-keyword-61973916d4,
// Landroval, Horizon Witness class: "target attacking creature without
// flying gains flying until end of turn."). Same before-the-switch
// narrowing shape as resolveAttackingSameDefenderAsSourceFilter above: drops
// any already-chosen target that isn't currently attacking or that already
// carries the named keyword, gated by EffectValue["target_filter"] ==
// "attacking_creature_without_keyword" (EffectValue["without_keyword"] names
// the keyword, e.g. "flying") so an ordinary "target attacking creature"
// grant with no such flag (negative example) is left completely untouched.
func (gs *GameState) resolveAttackingCreatureWithoutKeywordFilter(effectValue map[string]interface{}, targets []Target) []Target {
	filter, _ := effectValue["target_filter"].(string)
	if filter != "attacking_creature_without_keyword" {
		return targets
	}
	kwName, _ := effectValue["without_keyword"].(string)
	if kwName == "" {
		return targets
	}
	kw := Keyword(strings.Title(strings.ToLower(kwName)))
	out := targets[:0]
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil || !card.Attacking {
			continue
		}
		if card.HasKeywordWithEffects(kw, gs.EffectManager) {
			continue
		}
		out = append(out, t)
	}
	return out
}

// executeExileFromGraveyardTargetEffect implements the
// exile_target_from_graveyard primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-from-graveyard-3fefe932a6, Scour the Desert class):
// "Exile target creature card from your graveyard." Unlike
// executeExileFromGraveyardEffect's amount-based auto-select (no target,
// front-of-graveyard order) and executeExileEffect's battlefield-target
// removal, this moves a single player-CHOSEN graveyard card to exile.
// effectValue["whose"] selects which player's graveyard holds the legal
// target — "you" (default, the ability's controller) or "opponent" (first
// other player), mirroring executeExileFromGraveyardEffect's own "whose"
// convention. An optional effectValue["filter"] (matchesPermanentFilter's
// vocabulary, e.g. "creature") narrows what may be exiled. Mirrors
// executeReturnCardFromGraveyardSubsetToHand's GetCard/filter/RemoveCard
// idiom, just moving to Exile instead of Hand. No target, no matching
// player, an absent card, or a filter mismatch is a no-op — never an error
// (Rule 601.2c-style illegal/absent target tolerance).
func (gs *GameState) executeExileFromGraveyardTargetEffect(controller int, effectValue map[string]interface{}, targets []Target) error {
	if len(targets) == 0 || !targets[0].IsCard || targets[0].CardID == "" {
		return nil
	}
	whose := strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace(getStringValue(effectValue, "whose", "you")))
	var p *Player
	if whose == "opponent" {
		for i := range gs.Players {
			if i != controller {
				p = gs.Players[i]
				break
			}
		}
	} else if controller >= 0 && controller < len(gs.Players) {
		p = gs.Players[controller]
	}
	if p == nil {
		return nil
	}
	cardID := targets[0].CardID
	card, err := p.Graveyard.GetCard(cardID)
	if err != nil || card == nil {
		return nil
	}
	filter, _ := effectValue["filter"].(string)
	if filter != "" && !matchesPermanentFilter(card, filter) {
		return nil
	}
	card, err = p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(cardID)
	if err != nil || card == nil {
		return nil
	}
	p.Exile.AddCard(card)
	return nil
}

// executeReturnCreatureAndLandFromGraveyard implements the
// return_from_graveyard_multi_type primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-from-graveyard-multi-type-e59a25a846, Crop Sigil /
// Delirium class): "Return up to one target creature card and up to one
// target land card from your graveyard to your hand." Unlike
// executeReturnFromGraveyard's single auto-selected set, this atom names
// TWO independently optional targets of two different types at once —
// each target is only moved if it is still present in the controller's
// graveyard and matches its expected type (creature or land); a
// type-mismatched, already-gone, or omitted ("up to one") target is
// silently skipped rather than failing the whole effect (Rule 601.2c: an
// illegal individual target doesn't invalidate the others). Mirrors
// searchNamedCardMultiZone's GetAll/RemoveCard/AddCard idiom for moving a
// card between zones by ID.
func (gs *GameState) executeReturnCreatureAndLandFromGraveyard(controller int, targets []Target) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return errors.New("invalid controller")
	}
	graveyard := gs.Players[controller].Graveyard
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		var found *Card
		for _, c := range graveyard.GetAll() {
			if c.ID == t.CardID {
				found = c
				break
			}
		}
		if found == nil {
			continue
		}
		if !found.IsCreature() && found.Type != TypeLand {
			continue
		}
		card, err := graveyard.RemoveCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil {
			continue
		}
		gs.Players[controller].Hand.AddCard(card)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeReturnCardFromGraveyardSubsetToHand implements the
// return_card_from_graveyard_to_hand primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-card-from-graveyard-to-hand-01eaafe522, Midnight
// Tilling class): "Mill four cards, then you may return a permanent card
// from among them to your hand." Unlike executeReturnFromGraveyard's
// whole-graveyard auto-select, the eligible set here is an explicit
// caller-supplied ID SUBSET (effectValue["candidates"] — typically the
// card IDs a preceding mill/dig step just placed in the graveyard),
// further narrowed by an optional card-type filter
// (effectValue["filter"], matchesPermanentFilter's vocabulary, e.g.
// "permanent"). The actual pick is the caller-supplied target — mirrors
// executeReturnCreatureAndLandFromGraveyard's target-validates-against-
// zone-and-type shape — so an omitted target (the "may" was declined) or
// a target outside the subset/filter is a no-op, never an error (Rule
// 601.2c-style illegal/absent target tolerance).
func (gs *GameState) executeReturnCardFromGraveyardSubsetToHand(controller int, effectValue map[string]interface{}, targets []Target) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	if len(targets) == 0 || !targets[0].IsCard || targets[0].CardID == "" {
		return nil
	}
	cardID := targets[0].CardID

	var candidates []string
	switch v := effectValue["candidates"].(type) {
	case []string:
		candidates = v
	case []interface{}:
		for _, e := range v {
			if s, ok := e.(string); ok {
				candidates = append(candidates, s)
			}
		}
	}
	inSubset := false
	for _, id := range candidates {
		if id == cardID {
			inSubset = true
			break
		}
	}
	if !inSubset {
		return nil
	}

	player := gs.Players[controller]
	card, err := player.Graveyard.GetCard(cardID)
	if err != nil || card == nil {
		return nil
	}
	filter, _ := effectValue["filter"].(string)
	if filter != "" && !matchesPermanentFilter(card, filter) {
		return nil
	}
	card, err = player.Graveyard.RemoveCard(cardID)
	if err != nil {
		return nil
	}
	player.Hand.AddCard(card)
	return nil
}

// executeConditionalCopyEffect implements the
// copy_conditional_if_control_type primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-copy-conditional-if-control-type-3b07e9333c, Rule 603.4
// intervening-if — A-Mentor's Guidance class: "copy it if you control a
// planeswalker, Cleric, Druid, Shaman, Warlock, or Wizard."). Mirrors
// executeCopyEffect's signature/shape exactly, but first checks board
// state for a permanent — matched by card TYPE (e.g. "Planeswalker") or
// creature SUBTYPE (e.g. "Cleric") — controlled by controller; the copy is
// performed only when at least one requiredTypes entry matches, and is
// skipped entirely (no error, no copy) otherwise. A separate function from
// executeCopyEffect on purpose, so unconditional "copy that spell"
// triggers keep calling the unguarded executor untouched.
func (gs *GameState) executeConditionalCopyEffect(controller int, targets []Target, amount int, requiredTypes []string) error {
	if !gs.controlsPermanentMatchingType(controller, requiredTypes) {
		return nil
	}
	return gs.executeCopyEffect(controller, targets, amount)
}

// executeCopyTargetManaValueEqualsX implements the
// copy_target_mana_value_equals_x primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-copy-target-mana-value-equals-x-6767d08e4d, League Guildmage
// class: "{X}{R}, {T}: Copy target instant or sorcery spell you control
// with mana value X."). Mirrors executeCopyEffect's SpellID/CopySpell shape
// exactly, but adds an equality check against the generic mana actually
// paid to activate the ability — already resolved to a plain int in
// EffectValue["mana_value_x"] by the same dispatch chokepoint
// executeDestroyTargetWithManaValueEqualToX documents (dynamic_amount.go).
// A target spell whose ManaCost.CMC() doesn't match X, or that isn't found
// on the stack, is skipped rather than failing the whole effect — the same
// per-target partial-legality idiom executeDestroyTargetWithManaValueEqualToX
// uses, so an unconstrained "copy target instant or sorcery spell you
// control" (no mana-value-equals-X requirement) keeps going through the
// plain executeCopyEffect untouched. amount <= 0 defaults to 1, same
// convention as executeCopyEffect.
func (gs *GameState) executeCopyTargetManaValueEqualsX(controller int, targets []Target, amount int, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	if amount <= 0 {
		amount = 1
	}
	manaValueX := getIntValue(effectValue, "mana_value_x", -1)
	for _, target := range targets {
		if target.SpellID == "" {
			continue
		}
		obj, err := gs.Stack.GetByID(target.SpellID)
		if err != nil || obj.Card == nil {
			continue
		}
		if manaValueX < 0 || obj.Card.ManaCost.CMC() != manaValueX {
			continue
		}
		for i := 0; i < amount; i++ {
			if _, err := gs.CopySpell(target.SpellID, controller, nil); err != nil {
				break
			}
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeExileSourceFromGraveyardThenEffect implements the
// exile_source_from_graveyard primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-source-from-graveyard-d0578deb66, Council's
// Deliberation class): "you may exile this card from your graveyard. If
// you do, <effect>." The source is looked up by ID directly in the
// controller's OWN graveyard (Rule 400.4 — exile is a per-player zone;
// mirrors executeReturnCreatureAndLandFromGraveyard's RemoveCard/AddCard
// idiom for moving a card between zones by ID) and moved to Exile only if
// still present there — already gone (moved out by another effect first)
// means the exile does not happen. then runs ONLY when the exile actually
// occurred, modeling the "if you do" intervening-if (Rule 603.4):
// declining/failing the exile must silently skip the follow-up rather
// than run it anyway. The "may" itself resolves as always-taken here —
// exiling a card already in the graveyard to gain a follow-up effect is
// pure upside with no cost, so there is no real decision to model (unlike
// PendingChoice picks, which name a choice AMONG options).
func (gs *GameState) executeExileSourceFromGraveyardThenEffect(controller int, sourceID string, then func() error) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return errors.New("invalid controller")
	}
	graveyard := gs.Players[controller].Graveyard
	card, err := graveyard.RemoveCard(sourceID)
	if err != nil {
		return nil
	}
	gs.Players[controller].Exile.AddCard(card)
	if then == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return then()
}

// executeReturnFromGraveyardWithCounter implements the
// return_from_graveyard_with_counter primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-from-graveyard-with-counter-bcdf26f215, Persist
// class): "Return target nonlegendary creature card from your graveyard
// to the battlefield with a -1/-1 counter on it." Mirrors
// executeReturnCreatureAndLandFromGraveyard's RemoveCard/AddCard idiom for
// moving a card between zones by ID, but the destination is the
// battlefield rather than hand, and — unlike a plain reanimation — this
// atom also places the named counter (effectValue["counter_type"] /
// effectValue["counter_count"]) on the permanent as part of the SAME
// resolution, via AddCounters (counter_placement.go) so any Rule 614
// counter-placement replacement effects still apply uniformly. A missing
