// or non-positive counter_count is treated as "no counter" (plain
// reanimation, negative example), leaving the returned card counter-free
// exactly as before this primitive existed. A target already gone from
// the graveyard is silently skipped, matching every other partial-
// legality executor in this file.
type namedGraveyardReturnCounter struct {
	counterType CounterType
	count       int
}

func resolveGraveyardReturnCounters(effectValue map[string]interface{}) []namedGraveyardReturnCounter {
	if raw, ok := effectValue["counters"].([]interface{}); ok && len(raw) > 0 {
		var counters []namedGraveyardReturnCounter
		for _, item := range raw {
			spec, ok := item.(map[string]interface{})
			if !ok {
				continue
			}
			counterType, _ := spec["type"].(string)
			if counterType == "" {
				continue
			}
			count := getIntValue(spec, "count", 1)
			if count > 0 {
				counters = append(counters, namedGraveyardReturnCounter{
					counterType: CounterType(counterType),
					count:       count,
				})
			}
		}
		return counters
	}

	counterType, _ := effectValue["counter_type"].(string)
	counterCount := getIntValue(effectValue, "counter_count", 0)
	if counterType != "" && counterCount > 0 {
		return []namedGraveyardReturnCounter{{
			counterType: CounterType(counterType),
			count:       counterCount,
		}}
	}
	return nil
}

func (gs *GameState) executeReturnFromGraveyardWithCounter(controller int, targets []Target, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return errors.New("invalid controller")
	}
	counters := resolveGraveyardReturnCounters(effectValue)
	graveyard := gs.Players[controller].Graveyard
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := graveyard.RemoveCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		card.Controller = controller
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(card)
		for _, counter := range counters {
			gs.AddCounters(card, counter.counterType, counter.count)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeReturnFromGraveyardAllMatchingToBattlefield implements the
// return_from_graveyard_all_matching_to_battlefield primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-from-graveyard-all-matching-to-battlefiel-74b2b9a3c7,
// Primevals' Glorious Rebirth class): "Return all legendary permanent
// cards from your graveyard to the battlefield." Unlike
// executeReturnFromGraveyard and executeReturnFromGraveyardWithCounter,
// which always operate on an explicit target list, this primitive is
// UNTARGETED and UNBOUNDED: every card in controller's own graveyard
// matching effectValue["filter"] (matchesPermanentFilter's vocabulary,
// same "filter" key executeReturnCardFromGraveyardSubsetToHand already
// uses; empty/absent filter matches every card) returns to the
// battlefield in this single resolution, simultaneously — never just the
// first match. Matching is computed up front into an ID list before any
// RemoveCard call so mutating the graveyard mid-scan can't skip entries.
// A plain "return target legendary creature card ..." reanimation must
// keep going through executeReturnFromGraveyard's targeted shape
// unchanged; this atom is reached only when the card's Oracle text has no
// target at all.
func (gs *GameState) executeReturnFromGraveyardAllMatchingToBattlefield(controller int, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return errors.New("invalid controller")
	}
	filter, _ := effectValue["filter"].(string)
	player := gs.Players[controller]
	var matched []string
	for _, card := range player.Graveyard.GetAll() {
		if filter == "" || matchesPermanentFilter(card, filter) {
			matched = append(matched, card.ID)
		}
	}
	for _, id := range matched {
		card, err := player.Graveyard.RemoveCard(id)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		card.Controller = controller
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(card)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeTemporaryCharacteristicOverwriteEOTEffect implements the
// temporary_characteristic_overwrite_eot primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-temporary-characteristic-overwrite-eot-716addc305, Map-validated
// capability temporary_characteristic_overwrite_eot — Dance of the Skywise
// class: "Until end of turn, target creature you control becomes a blue
// Dragon Illusion with base power and toughness 4/4, loses all abilities,
// and gains flying."). Deliberately narrower than that full sentence: this
// primitive only overwrites color, subtypes, base power/toughness, and
// strips keyword abilities from the targeted creature — the separate
// "gains flying" clause stays mapped through the existing
// grant_keywords_until_eot primitive so a card combining both still
// resolves as two independent effect atoms (a target gaining only flying
// must never be widened into this primitive).
func (gs *GameState) executeTemporaryCharacteristicOverwriteEOTEffect(source *Card, controller int, targets []Target) error {
	if len(targets) == 0 || !targets[0].IsCard {
		return nil
	}
	target, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(targets[0].CardID)
	if err != nil || target == nil {
		return nil
	}
	target.SubTypes = []string{"Dragon", "Illusion"}
	target.Colors = []Color{Blue}
	newPower, newToughness := 4, 4
	target.BasePower = &newPower
	target.BaseToughness = &newToughness
	target.Keywords = nil
	return nil
}

// executeGrantColorUntilEOTEffect implements the grant_color_until_eot
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-grant-color-until-eot-23e34406ef, Map-
// validated capability grant_color_until_eot — Disciple of Kangee class:
// "Target creature gains flying and becomes blue until end of turn.").
// Deliberately split from the keyword half of that sentence, which stays
// mapped through the existing grant_keywords_until_eot primitive as its
// own effect atom — this atom only overwrites the target's Colors (Rule
// 105.5b "becomes [color]" replaces the color characteristic outright,
// same full-replace semantics executeTemporaryCharacteristicOverwriteEOTEffect
// above uses for its own Colors write), so a card combining both clauses
// still resolves as two independent atoms and neither one strips the
// other's grant (a keyword already present on the target, e.g. from a
// sibling grant_keywords_until_eot atom, is left untouched here).
func (gs *GameState) executeGrantColorUntilEOTEffect(controller int, source *Card, effectValue map[string]interface{}, targets []Target) error {
	if len(targets) == 0 || !targets[0].IsCard {
		return nil
	}
	colorStr, _ := effectValue["color"].(string)
	if colorStr == "" {
		return nil
	}
	target, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(targets[0].CardID)
	if err != nil || target == nil {
		return nil
	}
	target.Colors = []Color{Color(strings.ToUpper(colorStr))}
	return nil
}

// executeGrantProtectionFromChosenColorUntilEOTEffect implements the
// grant_protection_from_chosen_color_until_eot primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-grant-protection-from-chosen-color-until-eot-41010ba1db, Pale
// Wayfarer class: "Target creature gains protection from the color of its
// controller's choice until end of turn."). The color is chosen by the
// TARGET's controller, not the resolving controller — via
// chooseColorForProtectionByPlayer(target.Controller), the same
// "no interactive chooser reachable from this package" auto-pick idiom
// cardfns.ChooseColorAutoForPlayer uses elsewhere (most-represented color
// among the chooser's own permanents, WUBRG tie-break, defaulting to
// White). Appends straight to Card.Protection like
// cardfns.GrantProtectionUntilEOT does (HasProtectionFromCard reads that
// field directly and isn't effect-manager-aware) and strips it back off in
// a one-shot EventCleanup subscription.
func (gs *GameState) executeGrantProtectionFromChosenColorUntilEOTEffect(targets []Target) error {
	if len(targets) == 0 || !targets[0].IsCard {
		return nil
	}
	target, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(targets[0].CardID)
	if err != nil || target == nil {
		return nil
	}
	quality := protectionQualityForColor(gs.chooseColorForProtectionByPlayer(target.Controller))
	if quality == "" {
		return nil
	}
	target.Protection = append(target.Protection, quality)
	targetID := target.ID
	if gs.EventBus != nil {
		reverted := false
		gs.EventBus.Subscribe(EventCleanup, func(e *Event) {
			if reverted {
				return
			}
			reverted = true
			c, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(targetID)
			if err != nil || c == nil {
				return
			}
			for i, p := range c.Protection {
				if p == quality {
					c.Protection = append(c.Protection[:i], c.Protection[i+1:]...)
					break
				}
			}
		})
	}
	return nil
}

// chooseColorForProtectionByPlayer auto-picks a color for playerIdx: the
// color most represented among the permanents they control, WUBRG
// tie-broken, defaulting to White when they control no colored permanents.
// Mirrors cardfns.ChooseColorAutoForPlayer's logic (this package can't
// import cardfns).
func (gs *GameState) chooseColorForProtectionByPlayer(playerIdx int) Color {
	counts := map[Color]int{}
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || c.Controller != playerIdx {
			continue
		}
		for _, col := range c.Colors {
			counts[col]++
		}
	}
	best, bestN := White, 0
	for _, col := range []Color{White, Blue, Black, Red, Green} {
		if counts[col] > bestN {
			best, bestN = col, counts[col]
		}
	}
	return best
}

// protectionQualityForColor maps a basic Color to its ProtectionQuality
// enum value ("protection from white/blue/black/red/green").
func protectionQualityForColor(c Color) ProtectionQuality {
	switch c {
	case White:
		return ProtectionFromWhite
	case Blue:
		return ProtectionFromBlue
	case Black:
		return ProtectionFromBlack
	case Red:
		return ProtectionFromRed
	case Green:
		return ProtectionFromGreen
	}
	return ""
}

// controlsPermanentMatchingType reports whether controller controls a
// permanent whose card type (e.g. "Planeswalker") or creature subtype
// (e.g. "Cleric") case-insensitively matches one of want. Battlefield-only
// (Rule 603.4 "if you control" checks a controlled permanent, not cards in
// other zones).
func (gs *GameState) controlsPermanentMatchingType(controller int, want []string) bool {
	if gs.Battlefield == nil || len(want) == 0 {
		return false
	}
	for _, perm := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if perm == nil || perm.Controller != controller {
			continue
		}
		for _, w := range want {
			if strings.EqualFold(string(perm.Type), w) {
				return true
			}
			for _, st := range perm.SubTypes {
				if strings.EqualFold(st, w) {
					return true
				}
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

// resolvePumpAttachedCreatureNoTarget implements three allied no-target
// pump_until_eot primitives:
//
//   - pump_attached_creature_no_target (ticket
//     engine.auto-pump-attached-creature-no-target-6c04ae9e5b, Demonspine
//     Whip class: "{X}: Equipped creature gets +X/+0 until end of turn.")
//     — resolves against the ability's own source's ATTACHED creature
//     (Card.AttachedTo, an equipment/aura referent).
//   - pump_until_eot_implicit_self (ticket
//     engine.auto-pump-until-eot-implicit-self-ac9e168f84, Conifer Wurm
//     class: "{3}{G}: This creature gets +X/+X until end of turn, where X
//     is the number of snow permanents you control.") — when the source
//     isn't attached to anything but is itself a creature, "this
//     creature" means the source, so an untargeted pump_until_eot/pump
//     atom resolves against the source's own permanent instead of
//     no-oping. The dynamic amount (X) and until-end-of-turn duration are
//     untouched by this change — they're already produced by the existing
//     dynamic-amount and effect-duration machinery once a target is
//     present; this function only supplies that target.
//   - untargeted_shared_type_pump_until_eot (ticket
//     engine.auto-untargeted-shared-type-pump-until-eot-50f7b6ac08, Crown
//     of Suspicion class: "Sacrifice this Aura: Enchanted creature and
//     other creatures that share a creature type with it get +2/-1 until
//     end of turn."). Unlike the equipment case above, an AURA
//     (source.Type == TypeEnchantment) attached to a creature expands to
//     MULTIPLE targets: the enchanted creature itself plus every OTHER
//     battlefield creature sharing at least one SubTypes entry with it.
//     Gated on source.Type so Demonspine Whip's Artifact-equipment case
//     above is completely unaffected (that class has no known Aura
//     example, so the AttachedTo branch was previously equipment-only in
//     practice). If the enchanted creature can no longer be resolved on
//     the battlefield, the ability stays unmapped (empty targets) rather
//     than falling back to a stale, unresolved AttachedTo reference.
//
// All cases mirror executeTapSelfEffect's "read the referent straight off
// source, no target-filter selection" shape one primitive below. A genuine
// "target creature gets +X/+0" atom already carries a populated targets
// slice and is returned unchanged, so explicit-target pump effects keep
// resolving via targeting exactly as before, and any OTHER no-target
// effect name never reaches this function at all (the leading
// effect-name guard below).
func (gs *GameState) resolvePumpAttachedCreatureNoTarget(effect string, targets []Target, source *Card) []Target {
	if effect != "pump_until_eot" && effect != "pump" {
		return targets
	}
	if len(targets) != 0 || source == nil {
		return targets
	}
	if source.AttachedTo != "" {
		if source.Type == TypeEnchantment {
			enchanted, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(source.AttachedTo)
			if err != nil || enchanted == nil {
				return targets
			}
			result := []Target{{Type: TargetCreature, CardID: enchanted.ID, IsCard: true}}
			for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
				if c == nil || c.ID == enchanted.ID || !c.IsCreature() {
					continue
				}
				if sharesCreatureType(enchanted, c) {
					result = append(result, Target{Type: TargetCreature, CardID: c.ID, IsCard: true})
				}
			}
			return result
		}
		return []Target{{Type: TargetCreature, CardID: source.AttachedTo, IsCard: true}}
	}
	if source.IsCreature() {
		return []Target{{Type: TargetCreature, CardID: source.ID, IsCard: true}}
	}
	return targets
}

// sharesCreatureType reports whether a and b have at least one SubTypes
// entry in common (case-insensitive) — the "share a creature type" test
// Crown of Suspicion's class of Auras uses to select shared-type pump
// recipients.
func sharesCreatureType(a, b *Card) bool {
	if a == nil || b == nil {
		return false
	}
	for _, at := range a.SubTypes {
		for _, bt := range b.SubTypes {
			if strings.EqualFold(at, bt) {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

// executeTapSelfEffect implements the tap_self_no_target primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-tap-self-no-target-e2314dfde5, Map-validated capability
// tap_self_no_target — A-Soul of Windgrace class: "... Soul of Windgrace
// gains indestructible until end of turn. Tap it."). "It" refers to the
// ability's own source permanent, so unlike executeTapEffect's "tap" case
// (game/ability_effects.go) and tap_target_permanent/tap_target_creatures
// above, which all tap externally-selected members of a resolved Target
// list, this executor takes the source card directly and never touches the
// target-filter selection pipeline at all — there is no []Target parameter
// to resolve. Mirrors tap_target_permanent's full card.Tap() +
// EventTapped/EventBecomesTapped semantics so becomes_tapped triggers on
// the source still fire identically; a genuine "Tap target creature."
// clause is unaffected and keeps routing through the registered "tap"/
// tap_target_permanent primitives.
func (gs *GameState) executeTapSelfEffect(source *Card) error {
	if source == nil {
		return nil
	}
	source.Tap()
	if gs.EventBus != nil {
		gs.ProcessTriggeredAbilities(NewEvent(EventTapped).WithSource(source).WithPlayer(source.Controller))
		gs.ProcessTriggeredAbilities(NewEvent(EventBecomesTapped).WithSource(source).WithPlayer(source.Controller))
	}
	return nil
}

// executeBounceEnchantedAndSelf implements the bounce_enchanted_and_self
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-bounce-enchanted-and-self-d89efd9bb9,
// Disappear class): "Enchant creature / {U}: Return enchanted creature
// and this Aura to their owners' hands." A single resolution returns BOTH
// the non-targeted enchanted-creature referent (enchantedCreatureID,
// resolved by the caller the same way Untap's existing attached/
// enchanted-creature referent is resolved elsewhere — not re-derived
// here) and the source permanent itself (source.ID) to their respective
// owners' hands via the same gs.Bounce(id) primitive
// executeBounceFiltered already uses. Distinct from every
// target_filter-based bounce shape (blue_or_black_creature,
// other_creature, one-or-two target creatures), which only ever bounces
// the TARGETED creature(s) — this atom also bounces the SOURCE, on top of
// a referent that was never a target in the first place (Rule 601.2c:
// "enchanted creature" names an object by relationship, not targeting).
// A missing/already-gone referent (enchantedCreatureID == "") still lets
// the Aura itself bounce.
func (gs *GameState) executeBounceEnchantedAndSelf(source *Card, enchantedCreatureID string) error {
	if source == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if enchantedCreatureID != "" {
		gs.Bounce(enchantedCreatureID) //nolint:errcheck
	}
	gs.Bounce(source.ID) //nolint:errcheck
	return nil
}

// executeBounceAttachedAndItsAuras implements the
// bounce_attached_and_its_auras primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-bounce-attached-and-its-auras-c07cebd0f9, Mark of Eviction
// class): "At the beginning of your upkeep, return enchanted creature and
// all Auras attached to that creature to their owners' hands." Unlike
// executeBounceEnchantedAndSelf just above (a FIXED pair: the enchanted
// creature plus the one source Aura already known to the caller), this
// atom must discover EVERY Aura currently attached to the enchanted
// permanent — there can be more than one, and the triggering Aura itself
// is included since it too is attached to that creature — by scanning the
// battlefield for AttachedTo == enchantedCreatureID at resolution time,
// filtered to Auras only (Equipment sharing the same anchor is left
// alone, matching the Oracle text's "all Auras" wording). The attached
// Auras are bounced BEFORE the anchor itself: HandlePermanentLeavingBattlefield
// sends any still-attached Aura to the GRAVEYARD (Rule 704.5m
// state-based action) the moment the permanent it enchants leaves the
// battlefield, so bouncing the anchor first would silently turn "return
// ... to their owners' hands" into "die." Bouncing each attached Aura
// first, while the anchor is still on the battlefield, returns every one
// of them to hand via the same gs.Bounce primitive used above, and only
// then bounces the now-unattached anchor.
func (gs *GameState) executeBounceAttachedAndItsAuras(enchantedCreatureID string) error {
	if enchantedCreatureID == "" {
		return nil
	}
	auraIDs := []string{}
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || c.AttachedTo != enchantedCreatureID || !c.IsAura() {
			continue
		}
		auraIDs = append(auraIDs, c.ID)
	}
	for _, id := range auraIDs {
		gs.Bounce(id) //nolint:errcheck
	}
	gs.Bounce(enchantedCreatureID) //nolint:errcheck
	return nil
}

// executeReturnFromGraveyardAttachToTriggerObject implements the
// return_from_graveyard_attach_to_trigger_object primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-return-from-graveyard-attach-to-trigger-object-4decc87d92,
// Smoke Shroud class): "When a Ninja you control enters, you may return
// this card from your graveyard to the battlefield attached to that
// creature." Unlike the plain return_from_graveyard case (an
// independently chosen or filtered target), the attachment anchor here is
// fixed to the permanent that caused the trigger — anchor is
// stackObj.EventCard by the same convention
// executeDamageEqualToToughnessToControllerEffect below uses for "that
// creature." source is the card to reanimate, located in its owner's
// graveyard by the caller. Both AttachedTo (on source) and Attachments
// (on anchor) are set, mirroring how every other attach site in this
// engine links an Aura/Equipment to its anchor, and RegisterCardAbilities
// is called so source's own triggered/static abilities (e.g. its own Aura
// buff) come alive exactly as they would for any other permanent entering
// the battlefield. No-op if source or anchor is nil, anchor is not (or no
// longer) on the battlefield, or source is not (or no longer) in its
// owner's graveyard.
func (gs *GameState) executeReturnFromGraveyardAttachToTriggerObject(source *Card, anchor *Card) error {
	if source == nil || anchor == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if !gs.Battlefield.HasCard(anchor.ID) {
		return nil
	}
	if source.Owner < 0 || source.Owner >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	owner := gs.Players[source.Owner]
	if !owner.Graveyard.HasCard(source.ID) {
		return nil
	}
	card, err := owner.Graveyard.RemoveCard(source.ID)
	if err != nil {
		return nil
	}
	card.Controller = source.Owner
	card.AttachedTo = anchor.ID
	anchor.Attachments = append(anchor.Attachments, card.ID)
	gs.Battlefield.AddCard(card)
	gs.RegisterCardAbilities(card)
	return nil
}

// executeDamageEqualToToughnessToControllerEffect implements the
// damage_equal_to_toughness_to_controller primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-damage-equal-to-toughness-6597113781, Map-validated
// capability damage_equal_to_toughness — Creature Bond class: "When
// enchanted creature dies, this Aura deals damage equal to that
// creature's toughness to the creature's controller."). victim is the
// creature the triggering event pertains to (the Aura's "that creature"
// referent, carried as stackObj.EventCard by the caller) — its OWN
// toughness supplies the damage amount and its OWN controller is the
// fixed recipient, unlike every targeted "damage" case in the switch
// below, which resolves an externally chosen []Target list instead.
func (gs *GameState) executeDamageEqualToToughnessToControllerEffect(victim *Card) error {
	if victim == nil {
		return nil
	}
	amount := victim.GetToughness()
	if amount <= 0 {
		return nil
	}
	controller := victim.Controller
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	gs.Players[controller].Life -= amount
	return nil
}

// executeDamageAndGainLifeToControllerEffect implements the
// damage_gain_life_equal_to_damage_dealt primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-damage-effect-gain-life-equal-to-damage-dealt-5fd697d164,
// Enter the God-Eternals class: "~ deals N damage to target creature and
// you gain life equal to the damage dealt this way."). Applies amount to
// every target through the SAME DamageTargetFromSource protection/
// prevention/redirect pipeline every other targeted-damage primitive
// uses, then gains source's controller life equal to the ACTUAL damage
// dealt by this instance — measured as the delta in each recipient's own
// damage/DamageTakenThisTurn tracking so a Fog-style prevention shield
// (or protection) that reduces the damage to 0 correctly grants no life,
// rather than trusting the raw requested amount. Distinct from every
// plain "damage" primitive (executeDamageEqualToToughnessToControllerEffect
// above included), none of which touch the controller's life at all —
// per the ticket's negative example, a bare damage effect must never
// grant life on its own, so this is a dedicated primitive rather than a
// flag folded onto the generic damage path.
func (gs *GameState) executeDamageAndGainLifeToControllerEffect(targets []Target, amount int, source *Card) error {
	if source == nil || amount <= 0 {
		return nil
	}
	controller := source.Controller
	if controller < 0 || controller >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	total := 0
	for _, target := range targets {
		if target.IsCard {
			before := 0
			if card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID); err == nil && card != nil {
				before = card.Damage
			}
			if err := DamageTargetFromSource(target, amount, source, gs); err != nil {
				return err
			}
			if card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID); err == nil && card != nil {
				total += card.Damage - before
			}
			continue
		}
		if target.PlayerIdx < 0 || target.PlayerIdx >= len(gs.Players) {
			continue
		}
		before := gs.Players[target.PlayerIdx].DamageTakenThisTurn
		if err := DamageTargetFromSource(target, amount, source, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		total += gs.Players[target.PlayerIdx].DamageTakenThisTurn - before
	}
	if total > 0 {
		gs.Players[controller].GainLife(total)
	}
	return nil
}

// ExecuteDamagePerRecipientPermanentCountEffect implements the
// damage_amount_per_recipient_permanent_count primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-damage-amount-per-recipient-permanent-count-cd82a877db, Price
// of Progress class): "~ deals damage to each player equal to twice the
// number of nonbasic lands that player controls." Every amount_count spec
// kind in dynamic_amount.go resolves to ONE global int shared by every
// affected player — that shape cannot express "twice THAT player's own
// nonbasic lands" for a deals-damage-to-EACH-player effect, since two
// players with different nonbasic-land counts must take different amounts
// of damage from the SAME resolution. This atom instead loops gs.Players
// directly and recomputes the permanent count against EACH player's own
// battlefield permanents immediately before applying that same player's
// own damage, rather than resolving one shared amount up front and reusing
// it. Currently supports only the "nonbasic_lands" filter (the Price of
// Progress shape); any other/missing filter value is a no-op, matching the
// fail-closed convention other amount_count resolvers use for an
// unrecognized spec kind (a fixed-amount "deals 3 damage to each opponent"
// effect never routes through here, and a global X-based amount not tied
// to a per-recipient permanent count has no matching "filter" key).
// multiplier <= 0 is also a no-op.
func (gs *GameState) ExecuteDamagePerRecipientPermanentCountEffect(effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	multiplier := getIntValue(effectValue, "multiplier", 1)
	if multiplier <= 0 {
		return nil
	}
	filter, _ := effectValue["filter"].(string)
	if filter != "nonbasic_lands" {
		return nil
	}
	if gs.Battlefield == nil {
		return nil
	}
	for i, p := range gs.Players {
		if p == nil {
			continue
		}
		count := 0
		for _, perm := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
			if perm == nil || perm.Controller != i || perm.Type != TypeLand || perm.IsBasic {
				continue
			}
			count++
		}
		amount := multiplier * count
		if amount <= 0 {
			continue
		}
		p.Life -= amount
	}
	return nil
}

// executeDestroyTargetThenGainLifeEffect implements the
// destroy_target_then_gain_life primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-destroy-target-then-gain-life-ae43167ca6, Vengeant Vampire
// class: "destroy target creature an opponent controls and you gain 4
// life."). One triggered effect, two sub-effects, neither dropped: each
// supplied card target is destroyed through the SAME DestroyTarget
// primitive every other single-target destroy uses (so regeneration,
// protection, and death triggers behave identically to a plain destroy),
// and the ability's controller gains a FIXED amount of life via the
// event-publishing GainLife helper. The life amount is read from
// effectValue["amount"] (the fixed number the reparse converter emits);
// when it is absent or non-positive the effect still performs the destroy
// but grants no life, so a mis-mapped record degrades to the plain
// destroy path rather than silently dropping the destroy.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyTargetThenGainLifeEffect(targets []Target, effectValue map[string]interface{}, controller int) error {
	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		if err := DestroyTarget(target, gs); err != nil {
			continue
		}
	}
	amount := getIntValue(effectValue, "amount", 0)
	if amount > 0 && controller >= 0 && controller < len(gs.Players) {
		gs.GainLife(controller, amount)
	}
	return nil
}

// HeterogeneousTokenSpec describes a single token to create as part of the
// create_heterogeneous_creature_tokens primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-create-heterogeneous-creature-tokens-e7352ae77c, Map-validated
// capability create_heterogeneous_creature_tokens — Bestial Menace class:
// "Create a 1/1 green Snake creature token, a 2/2 green Wolf creature
// token, and a 3/3 green Elephant creature token."). Unlike the existing
// single-shape create_token effect, whose Value map describes ONE token
// shape (optionally repeated Count times), Oracle text in this family lists
// several DIFFERENT token shapes in a single sentence, each with its own
// power, toughness, color and subtype that must survive independently
// rather than being coerced into one generic token.
type HeterogeneousTokenSpec struct {
	Name      string
	SubType   string
	Color     Color
	Power     int
	Toughness int
}

// executeCreateHeterogeneousCreatureTokensEffect creates one creature token
// per entry in specs under controller's control, each preserving that
// entry's own power, toughness, color and subtype, and returns the created
// tokens in the same order as specs. A no-op (nil, nil) if the battlefield
// isn't initialized or specs is empty. Reuses NewCreature for the base
// card so each token's power/toughness are constructed the same way any
// other test/engine creature is, rather than duplicating that construction
// logic here.
func (gs *GameState) executeCreateHeterogeneousCreatureTokensEffect(controller int, specs []HeterogeneousTokenSpec) ([]*Card, error) {
	if gs.Battlefield == nil || len(specs) == 0 {
		return nil, nil
	}
	tokens := make([]*Card, 0, len(specs))
	for _, spec := range specs {
		token := NewCreature(spec.Name, ManaCost{}, spec.Power, spec.Toughness, 0)
		token.SubTypes = []string{spec.SubType}
		token.Colors = []Color{spec.Color}
		token.Owner = controller
		token.Controller = controller
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(token)
		tokens = append(tokens, token)
	}
	return tokens, nil
}

// TokenFullDefinitionSpec describes a single creature token shape to
// create — power/toughness, color, subtype, and keyword abilities — as
// part of the create_token_full_definition primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-create-token-full-definition-eb88249643, Map-validated
// capability create_token_full_definition — Crowded Crypt class: "Create a
// 2/2 black Zombie creature token with decayed for each corpse counter on
// this artifact."). Unlike HeterogeneousTokenSpec above (several DIFFERENT
// token shapes in one sentence, no keywords), this is ONE token shape
// repeated Count times that also carries keyword abilities (e.g.
// "decayed") baked onto every copy created.
type TokenFullDefinitionSpec struct {
	Name      string
	SubType   string
	Color     Color
	Power     int
	Toughness int
	Keywords  []Keyword
	Count     int
}

// executeCreateTokenFullDefinitionEffect creates spec.Count copies of a
// single creature token shape under controller's control, each stamped
// with spec's power, toughness, color, subtype and keyword abilities, and
// returns the created tokens. A no-op (nil, nil) if the battlefield isn't
// initialized, spec.Count <= 0, or spec.Name is empty. Mirrors
// executeCreateHeterogeneousCreatureTokensEffect's NewCreature-based
// construction, extended with a Keywords stamp so keyword-bearing token
// shapes like Crowded Crypt's decayed Zombies don't need a bespoke
// per-card handler.
func (gs *GameState) executeCreateTokenFullDefinitionEffect(controller int, spec TokenFullDefinitionSpec) ([]*Card, error) {
	if gs.Battlefield == nil || spec.Count <= 0 || spec.Name == "" {
		return nil, nil
	}
	tokens := make([]*Card, 0, spec.Count)
	for i := 0; i < spec.Count; i++ {
		token := NewCreature(spec.Name, ManaCost{}, spec.Power, spec.Toughness, 0)
		token.SubTypes = []string{spec.SubType}
		token.Colors = []Color{spec.Color}
		if len(spec.Keywords) > 0 {
			token.Keywords = append(token.Keywords, spec.Keywords...)
		}
		token.Owner = controller
		token.Controller = controller
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(token)
		tokens = append(tokens, token)
	}
	return tokens, nil
}

// executeExileTargetCardsSingleGraveyardEffect implements the
// exile_target_cards_single_graveyard primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-graveyard-card-target-ca4c0bd5ac, REPARSE-MAP
// verb_unmapped:exile / "Exile up to two target cards from a single
// graveyard" family — Famished Ghoul class): unlike
// exile_target_cards_from_graveyard below, whose targets are already
// constrained to one player by the Oracle text itself (Lochmere Serpent's
// "an opponent's graveyard"), this shape's Oracle text imposes the
// single-graveyard constraint on the CHOSEN SET of targets: all of them
// must come from the same graveyard, whichever one that is. The executor
// enforces this at resolution time: it locks onto whichever graveyard
// holds the FIRST resolvable target, then only exiles targets also found
// in that same player's graveyard — a target sitting in a different
// graveyard is skipped rather than exiled, exactly as if it had been an
// illegal target. Battlefield permanents are never consulted here
// (executeExileEffect's battlefield resolution is unaffected). A vanished
// target (Rule 608.2b) is skipped like any other; if none of the targets
// resolve to a graveyard at all, this is a no-op.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileTargetCardsSingleGraveyardEffect(targets []Target) error {
	ownerIdx := -1
	for _, tgt := range targets {
		if !tgt.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		for i, p := range gs.Players {
			if p == nil {
				continue
			}
			if card, err := p.Graveyard.GetCard(tgt.CardID); err == nil && card != nil {
				ownerIdx = i
				break
			}
		}
		if ownerIdx != -1 {
			break
		}
	}
	if ownerIdx < 0 || ownerIdx >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	p := gs.Players[ownerIdx]
	for _, tgt := range targets {
		if !tgt.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		card, err := p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(tgt.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		p.Exile.AddCard(card)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeExileTargetCardsFromEventPlayerGraveyardEffect implements the
// auto_exile_cards_from_specific_player_graveyard_d4263a7f1f primitive
// (ticket engine.auto-exile-cards-from-specific-player-graveyard-d4263a7f1f,
// Skullsnatcher class: "Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a
// player, exile up to two target cards from that player's graveyard.").
// Unlike executeExileTargetCardsSingleGraveyardEffect above, which locks
// onto whichever graveyard holds the FIRST resolvable target (an
// undirected "a single graveyard" reading), this shape's Oracle text names
// a SPECIFIC player's graveyard — the one identified dynamically by the
// trigger context (the combat-damage recipient), carried on
// StackObject.EventPlayer/HasEventPlayer exactly like the existing
// target_ref=="event_player" rewrite (above) uses for player-typed
// targets. Since the targets here are CARDS rather than the player
// itself, that generic rewrite doesn't apply; this executor instead reads
// EventPlayer directly and exiles only the targeted cards that are
// actually in THAT player's graveyard, skipping any target that isn't
// there (fizzled elsewhere, or stale from a different graveyard) exactly
// like a vanished target (Rule 608.2b). No stamped event player: no-op.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileTargetCardsFromEventPlayerGraveyardEffect(stackObj *StackObject, targets []Target) error {
	if stackObj == nil || !stackObj.HasEventPlayer || stackObj.EventPlayer < 0 || stackObj.EventPlayer >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	p := gs.Players[stackObj.EventPlayer]
	if p == nil {
		return nil
	}
	for _, tgt := range targets {
		if !tgt.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		card, err := p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(tgt.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		p.Exile.AddCard(card)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeExileGraveyardEffect implements the exile_graveyard primitive
// (ticket engine.auto-exile-graveyard-cc012546ee, REPARSE-MAP
// verb_unmapped:exile / "exile target player's graveyard" family — Bojuka
// Bog class): unlike executeExileTargetCardsSingleGraveyardEffect above
// (card targets) and executeExileAllGraveyardsEffect below (untargeted,
// every player), this shape targets a PLAYER directly (Rule 115.1a) and
// empties that one player's entire graveyard into their own exile zone.
// A vanished target (Rule 608.2b, no resolvable player target) is a
// no-op.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileGraveyardEffect(targets []Target) error {
	playerIdx := -1
	for _, tgt := range targets {
		if tgt.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		if tgt.PlayerIdx >= 0 && tgt.PlayerIdx < len(gs.Players) {
			playerIdx = tgt.PlayerIdx
			break
		}
	}
	if playerIdx < 0 {
		return nil
	}
	p := gs.Players[playerIdx]
	if p == nil {
		return nil
	}
	ids := make([]string, 0, len(p.Graveyard.Cards))
	for _, c := range p.Graveyard.Cards {
		ids = append(ids, c.ID)
	}
	for _, id := range ids {
		card, err := p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(id)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		p.Exile.AddCard(card)
	}
	return nil
}

// executeExileTargetPlayersGraveyardsEffect implements the
// exile_target_players_graveyards primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-target-players-graveyards-ba39c08200, Stonespeaker
// Crystal class): "Exile any number of target players' graveyards."
// Unlike executeExileGraveyardEffect above (locked to the FIRST player
// target found, via break) and executeExileAllGraveyardsEffect /
// executeExileEachOpponentsGraveyardEffect (untargeted, every player or
// every opponent), this shape resolves EVERY player Target present, each
// into that same player's own exile zone, as one effect. Zero targets
// (declining "any number of") is legal and a no-op; a vanished target
// (Rule 608.2b) is simply skipped.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileTargetPlayersGraveyardsEffect(targets []Target) error {
	for _, tgt := range targets {
		if tgt.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		if tgt.PlayerIdx < 0 || tgt.PlayerIdx >= len(gs.Players) {
			continue
		}
		p := gs.Players[tgt.PlayerIdx]
		if p == nil || p.Graveyard == nil {
			continue
		}
		cards := append([]*Card(nil), p.Graveyard.Cards...)
		for _, c := range cards {
			if c == nil {
				continue
			}
			card, err := p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(c.ID)
			if err != nil || card == nil {
				continue
			}
			p.Exile.AddCard(card)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// MonarchChangeExile records a single permanent exiled via the
// exile_until_monarch_change primitive (see
// executeExileUntilMonarchChangeEffect / CheckMonarchChangeExileReturns
// immediately below): Controller is the player who exiled the card, whose
// OWN opponent becoming the monarch is the return condition — NOT the
// current monarch at exile time.
type MonarchChangeExile struct {
	CardID     string
	OwnerIdx   int
	Controller int
}

// executeExileUntilMonarchChangeEffect implements the
// exile_until_monarch_change primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-until-monarch-change-f76e2a831d, Palace Jailer class):
// "exile target creature an opponent controls until an opponent becomes
// the monarch." Unlike executeDurableSelfExileEffect above (return keyed to
// the SOURCE leaving the battlefield) or a plain exile with no return
// clause, this duration is keyed to Rule 718's monarch designation: the
// exiled permanent returns to the battlefield under its owner's control the
// moment gs.MonarchIdx names anyone other than controller (the player who
// exiled it) — i.e. an OPPONENT of controller becomes the monarch. The
// pending return is recorded on GameState.MonarchChangeExiles
// (gamestate.go) and resolved by CheckMonarchChangeExileReturns below. A
// vanished target (Rule 608.2b, no resolvable battlefield permanent) is a
// no-op.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileUntilMonarchChangeEffect(target Target, controller int) error {
	if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
		return nil
	}
	card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
	if err != nil || card == nil {
		return nil
	}
	owner := card.Owner
	if owner < 0 || owner >= len(gs.Players) {
		return nil
	}
	gs.Battlefield.RemoveCard(card.ID)
	gs.UnregisterCardAbilities(card)
	gs.Players[owner].Exile.AddCard(card)
	gs.MonarchChangeExiles = append(gs.MonarchChangeExiles, MonarchChangeExile{
		CardID:     card.ID,
		OwnerIdx:   owner,
		Controller: controller,
	})
	return nil
}

// CheckMonarchChangeExileReturns resolves every pending
// exile_until_monarch_change return recorded by
// executeExileUntilMonarchChangeEffect above: any exiled permanent whose
// recorded Controller is not the CURRENT monarch (gs.MonarchIdx >= 0, i.e.
// someone actually holds the designation, and that someone isn't
// Controller — an OPPONENT of Controller became monarch) comes back to the
// battlefield under its owner's control, and its record is cleared. If
// Controller themself is (or becomes) the monarch, the exile persists — the
// Oracle text is keyed to an OPPONENT becoming monarch, not any monarch
// change. A card no longer present in its owner's exile zone (moved
// elsewhere by another effect in the meantime) is silently dropped without
// returning anything, mirroring the vanished-target convention used
// throughout this file.
func (gs *GameState) CheckMonarchChangeExileReturns() {
	if gs.MonarchIdx < 0 || len(gs.MonarchChangeExiles) == 0 {
		return
	}
	remaining := gs.MonarchChangeExiles[:0]
	for _, rec := range gs.MonarchChangeExiles {
		if gs.MonarchIdx == rec.Controller {
			remaining = append(remaining, rec)
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Players[rec.OwnerIdx].Exile.RemoveCard(rec.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		card.Controller = card.Owner
		gs.Battlefield.AddCard(card)
		gs.RegisterCardAbilities(card)
	}
	gs.MonarchChangeExiles = remaining
}

// executeExileAllGraveyardsEffect implements the exile_all_graveyards
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-exile-all-graveyards-d55e2dda8a,
// REPARSE-MAP verb_unmapped:exile — Abstergo Entertainment class: "...
// then exile all graveyards."): an untargeted mass zone effect that empties
// EVERY player's graveyard into that same player's exile zone, in a single
// resolution step. Unlike executeExileTargetCardsSingleGraveyardEffect
// above (target-driven, locked to one graveyard) and the battlefield-target
// executeExileEffect path, this takes no targets and no controller — it is
// unconditional and independent of any other clause on the same ability, so
// it is a separate function rather than a variant of either.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileAllGraveyardsEffect() error {
	for _, p := range gs.Players {
		if p == nil || p.Graveyard == nil {
			continue
		}
		cards := append([]*Card(nil), p.Graveyard.Cards...)
		for _, c := range cards {
			if c == nil {
				continue
			}
			card, err := p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(c.ID)
			if err != nil || card == nil {
				continue
			}
			p.Exile.AddCard(card)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeExileFromGraveyardEffect implements the exile_from_graveyard
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-exile-from-graveyard-f671ca6575, Kroxa and
// Kunoros class): "you may exile five cards from your graveyard" as a
// standalone zone-move effect, distinct from executeExileEffect
// (battlefield-target removal, Rule-608.2b fizzle semantics don't apply
// here) and the unrelated exileFromGraveyard(card, playerIdx) helper in
// activated_abilities.go, which pays a cost by exiling one NAMED card.
// effectValue["whose"] selects the referenced player — "you" (default, the
// ability's controller) or "opponent" (first other player) — mirroring the
// "whose" convention exile_all_from_library above uses; effectValue["amount"]
// is the (already dynamic-amount-resolved) card count. Auto-selects from the
// front of the graveyard, the same auto-select convention
// executeSearchLibraryXAmountToHand (effects.go) uses for its own X-based
// zone move, since the engine has no interactive per-card selection UI.
// Exiling fewer than amount (an undersized graveyard) is not an error —
// Rule 608.2b's "as much as possible" idiom. Returns the actual number of
// cards exiled so a linked "when you do" trigger can read it. No resolvable
// player or amount <= 0: no-op, 0 exiled.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileFromGraveyardEffect(controller int, effectValue map[string]interface{}) (int, error) {
	whose := strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace(getStringValue(effectValue, "whose", "you")))
	var p *Player
	if whose == "opponent" {
		for i := range gs.Players {
			if i != controller {
				p = gs.Players[i]
				break
			}
		}
	} else if controller >= 0 && controller < len(gs.Players) {
		p = gs.Players[controller]
	}
	if p == nil {
		return 0, nil
	}
	amount := 0
	switch v := effectValue["amount"].(type) {
	case int:
		amount = v
	case float64:
		amount = int(v)
	}
	if amount <= 0 {
		return 0, nil
	}
	cards := p.Graveyard.GetAll()
	if amount > len(cards) {
		amount = len(cards)
	}
	ids := make([]string, 0, amount)
	for i := 0; i < amount; i++ {
		if cards[i] != nil {
			ids = append(ids, cards[i].ID)
		}
	}
	exiled := 0
	for _, id := range ids {
		card, err := p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(id)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		p.Exile.AddCard(card)
		exiled++
	}
	return exiled, nil
}

// executeExileEachOpponentsGraveyardEffect implements the
// exile_each_opponents_graveyard primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-exile-each-opponents-graveyard-8ef3f799a1, REPARSE-MAP
// verb_unmapped:exile — Dauntless Scrapbot class: "When this creature
// enters, exile each opponent's graveyard."): an ETB effect that empties
// EVERY OPPONENT's graveyard into that same opponent's own exile zone,
// leaving the controller's own graveyard untouched. Unlike
// executeExileAllGraveyardsEffect above (every player including the
// controller) and executeExileGraveyardEffect (a single targeted player),
// this is untargeted but scoped to controller's opponents only.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileEachOpponentsGraveyardEffect(controller int) error {
	for i, p := range gs.Players {
		if p == nil || i == controller || p.Graveyard == nil {
			continue
		}
		cards := append([]*Card(nil), p.Graveyard.Cards...)
		for _, c := range cards {
			if c == nil {
				continue
			}
			card, err := p.Graveyard.RemoveCard(c.ID)
			if err != nil || card == nil {
				continue
			}
			p.Exile.AddCard(card)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// ============================================================================
// EFFECT EXECUTOR
// ============================================================================

// resolveModalChoiceEffect implements the modal_choice_effect primitive
// (Lava Storm class: "~ deals 2 damage to each attacking creature or ~
// deals 2 damage to each blocking creature"): at resolution the caster
// chooses exactly one of two alternative effect clauses in the Oracle
// text, and only that chosen clause executes — the other clause and its
// recipients are never touched. EffectValue["modal_choice"] carries
// "modes" (exactly two {"effect","value"} clause maps, closed shape) and
// "chosen" (the already-decided 0/1 mode index — mirrors how
// "amount_from"/"x_amount_count" elsewhere in this file already assume
// the printed X/announced choice is resolved before this function runs;
// collecting the choice from a real player is a separate, out-of-scope
// concern). Returns changed=false — leaving effect/effectValue untouched
// — when the key is absent or malformed, so a plain non-modal effect is
// never affected by this fold.
func (gs *GameState) resolveModalChoiceEffect(effect string, effectValue map[string]interface{}) (string, map[string]interface{}, bool) {
	mc, ok := effectValue["modal_choice"].(map[string]interface{})
	if !ok {
		return effect, effectValue, false
	}
	modes, ok := mc["modes"].([]interface{})
	if !ok || len(modes) != 2 {
		return effect, effectValue, false
	}
	chosen := 0
	switch v := mc["chosen"].(type) {
	case int:
		chosen = v
	case float64:
		chosen = int(v)
	}
	if chosen != 0 && chosen != 1 {
		chosen = 0
	}
	mode, ok := modes[chosen].(map[string]interface{})
	if !ok {
		return effect, effectValue, false
	}
	newEffect, ok := mode["effect"].(string)
	if !ok || newEffect == "" {
		return effect, effectValue, false
	}
	newValue, _ := mode["value"].(map[string]interface{})
	if newValue == nil {
		newValue = map[string]interface{}{}
	}
	return newEffect, newValue, true
}

// ResolveTriggerReferentPlayerController implements the
// trigger_referent_player_controller primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-trigger-referent-player-controller-04ad7093d0, Nature's Will
// class: "Whenever one or more creatures you control deal combat damage to
// a player, tap all lands THAT PLAYER controls and untap all lands you
// control."). The trigger condition introduces an unnamed player (the one
// dealt combat damage) that a later effect atom in the SAME ability refers
// back to anaphorically ("that player") — distinct both from the ability's
// own controller ("you control") and from an explicitly chosen ability
// target ("target player controls"). The atom marks the anaphoric
// reference via effectValue["controller_ref"] == "trigger_referent_player";
// this resolves it to eventPlayer (the player identified by the trigger
// event) instead of the ability's controller. Any other controller_ref
// value — including unset, and the separate "target_player" marker the
// explicit-target controller path uses — falls straight through to
// controller unchanged, so neither negative example ("target player
// controls" or "you control") is ever rerouted through this referent path.
// Also falls through when eventPlayer is negative (no referent identified),
// failing closed to the ability's own controller rather than an invalid
// player index.
func (gs *GameState) ResolveTriggerReferentPlayerController(effectValue map[string]interface{}, controller int, eventPlayer int) int {
	if effectValue == nil {
		return controller
	}
	ref, _ := effectValue["controller_ref"].(string)
	if ref != "trigger_referent_player" || eventPlayer < 0 {
		return controller
	}
	return eventPlayer
}

// resolveRecipientFromEventCard implements the
// damage_recipient_referent_controller AND damage_to_trigger_referent_creature
// primitives. Magnetic Mine class ("Whenever another artifact is put into a
// graveyard from the battlefield, ~ deals 2 damage to that artifact's
// controller."): the recipient is the CONTROLLER of the permanent the
// trigger condition itself referenced. Ashmouth Hound class ("Whenever this
// creature blocks or becomes blocked by a creature, this creature deals 1
// damage to that creature."): the recipient is the referent CREATURE
// ITSELF. Either way the referent comes from StackObject.EventCard — the
// same field event_subject_power/event_subject_toughness already read for
// stat-based amounts, dynamic_amount.go — not a target chosen at
// cast/activation, not "each opponent", and not a targeted creature's
// controller. EffectValue["recipient_from"]=="event_subject_controller"
// swaps in a synthetic single-player Target for that controller;
// =="event_subject" swaps in a synthetic single-card Target for the
// referent creature itself. Either substitution discards whatever targets
// (if any) were already present — this recipient is never itself a target,
// so it never goes through targeting/hexproof/protection checks, matching
// Oracle text that never says "target". Returns targets unchanged when the
// key is absent, unrecognized, or there is no event card to read a
// referent from, so a plain damage effect (including the real
// "attacking_or_blocking_creature" target-filter path) is never affected
// by this fold.
//
// This also folds the AMOUNT side of the same referent (ticket
// engine.auto-damage-amount-from-referent-card-property-d624b40f40,
// Hellhole Rats class: "target player discards a card. This creature
// deals damage to that player equal to that card's mana value."):
// EffectValue["amount_source"] == {"referent": ..., "property":
// "mana_value"} sets EffectValue["amount"] to eventCard's mana value
// (Card.ManaCost.CMC(), the same read resolvePumpReferentSpellManaValue
// above uses for its own referent-card mana-value fold) — folded in here
// rather than a separate resolver because this function is already the
// one call site that receives both effectValue and eventCard for a
// referent-card damage atom, and mutating the map is enough since Go
// maps are reference types. A no-op (leaves "amount" untouched) when
// amount_source is absent or its property is unrecognized, so a literal-
// amount damage atom (including one that still sets recipient_from) is
// never affected by this fold.
//
// EffectValue["amount_source"] == {"referent": "target", "property":
// "hand_size"} implements the damage_amount_referent_target_hand_size
// primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-damage-amount-referent-target-hand-size-5b58aa258c, Storm
// Seeker class: "~ deals damage to target player equal to the number of
// cards in that player's hand."). Unlike the mana_value fold above, this
// referent is the resolved TARGET (the []Target this function already
// receives), not eventCard, so it applies even when eventCard is nil — a
// plain targeted spell, not a triggered ability. Reads the hand size of
// the first TargetPlayer entry in targets AT FOLD TIME (i.e. at
// resolution, since targets are already locked in by then), never the
// source's own controller's hand (that stays the separate "hand"/
// "whose":"you" count spec, dynamic_amount.go) and never a caster's
// library size, per the ticket's negative examples. No player target
// present -> folds to 0, an honest no-op rather than a guessed amount.
func (gs *GameState) resolveRecipientFromEventCard(effectValue map[string]interface{}, eventCard *Card, targets []Target) []Target {
	if effectValue != nil {
		if amountSource, ok := effectValue["amount_source"].(map[string]interface{}); ok {
			switch amountSource["property"] {
			case "mana_value":
				if eventCard != nil {
					effectValue["amount"] = eventCard.ManaCost.CMC()
				}
			case "hand_size":
				if amountSource["referent"] == "target" {
					effectValue["amount"] = gs.handSizeOfFirstPlayerTarget(targets)
				}
			}
		}
	}
	recipientFrom, ok := effectValue["recipient_from"].(string)
	if !ok {
		return targets
	}
	if recipientFrom == "referent_target_controller" {
		return gs.resolveReferentTargetController(targets)
	}
	if eventCard == nil {
		return targets
	}
	switch recipientFrom {
	case "event_subject_controller":
		return []Target{{Type: TargetPlayer, PlayerIdx: eventCard.Controller}}
	case "event_subject":
		return []Target{{IsCard: true, CardID: eventCard.ID}}
	case "attacked_defender":
		// damage_to_attacked_defender primitive (ticket
		// engine.auto-damage-to-attacked-defender-b1aef0b736, Raid
		// Bombardment class: "~ deals 1 damage to the player or
		// planeswalker that creature is attacking"). eventCard here is
		// the ATTACKING CREATURE itself (the "attacks" trigger's
		// event.Source), not the recipient — its own AttackTarget (Rule
		// 506.4c) names the planeswalker it's attacking, if any;
		// otherwise it's attacking the defending player directly, which
		// this two-player engine always identifies as the attacker's
		// controller's opponent (the same 1-playerIdx convention
		// DeclareAttackersWithTargets itself uses).
		if eventCard.AttackTarget != nil {
			return []Target{{IsCard: true, CardID: eventCard.AttackTarget.ID}}
		}
		return []Target{{Type: TargetPlayer, PlayerIdx: 1 - eventCard.Controller}}
	case "event_subject_spell":
		// copy_triggering_referent primitive (ticket
		// engine.auto-copy-triggering-referent-b4a4facffb, Storm King's
		// Thunder class: "When you next cast an instant or sorcery spell
		// this turn, copy that spell X times."): "that spell" is an
		// implicit referent — eventCard itself, the spell that triggered
		// the delayed ability — resolved as a SpellID target so the
		// existing copy executor (game/copy_effect.go executeCopyEffect)
		// reaches its target.SpellID != "" branch and calls CopySpell,
		// exactly like an explicit "target instant or sorcery spell"
		// shape, without requiring a player to choose a target. Amount
		// ("X times") is unaffected by this fold — it already arrives
		// pre-resolved in effectValue["amount"] via the same dynamic-X
		// machinery documented on executeReturnFromGraveyardManaValueX
		// above, and executeCopyEffect already loops amount times.
		return []Target{{SpellID: eventCard.ID}}
	default:
		return targets
	}
}

// resolveReferentTargetController implements the damage_referent_controller
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-damage-referent-controller-e1946d49cc, Sonic
// Assault class: "Tap target creature. Sonic Assault deals 2 damage to that
// creature's controller."). Unlike resolveRecipientFromEventCard's other
// branches, this referent is not a triggered ability's StackObject.EventCard
// — it is the creature chosen by an EARLIER clause of the SAME spell/ability
// ("target creature"), carried here as the ability's own already-resolved
// targets list (every effect atom of a single ability resolves against the
// same Targets set at cast/activation time). Each card target is swapped for
// a synthetic player target pointing at that card's CURRENT controller (read
// live from the battlefield, not a snapshot, since an intervening effect in
// the same chain could change control of the referent before this atom
// runs). Falls back to the original targets unchanged if none of them
// resolve to a battlefield card, so a malformed or already-departed referent
// never silently turns into an empty target list.
func (gs *GameState) resolveReferentTargetController(targets []Target) []Target {
	resolved := make([]Target, 0, len(targets))
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		resolved = append(resolved, Target{Type: TargetPlayer, PlayerIdx: card.Controller})
	}
	if len(resolved) == 0 {
		return targets
	}
	return resolved
}

// handSizeOfFirstPlayerTarget returns the number of cards in the hand of
// the first TargetPlayer entry in targets — the "target player" referent
// resolveRecipientFromEventCard folds for
// damage_amount_referent_target_hand_size above. Returns 0 when targets
// contains no player target (honest no-op, never a guessed amount).
func (gs *GameState) handSizeOfFirstPlayerTarget(targets []Target) int {
	for _, t := range targets {
		if t.Type == TargetPlayer && t.PlayerIdx >= 0 && t.PlayerIdx < len(gs.Players) {
			return len(gs.Players[t.PlayerIdx].Hand.Cards)
		}
	}
	return 0
}

// resolvePumpReferentSpellManaValue implements the
// pump_referent_dynamic_amount_until_eot primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-pump-referent-dynamic-amount-until-eot-b195cfdc04, Erratic
// Cyclops class: "Whenever you cast an instant or sorcery spell, this
// creature gets +X/+0 until end of turn, where X is that spell's mana
// value."). eventCard is the triggering cast spell (StackObject.EventCard,
// the same referent field resolveRecipientFromEventCard reads above);
// source is this ability's own permanent — the "this creature" referent
// that always receives the pump, never an externally chosen target. When
// EffectValue["power_from"] == "referent_spell_mana_value" (closed, single
// value), this sets "power" to that spell's mana value and redirects
// targets to a single synthetic Target pointing at source, discarding
// whatever targets (if any) were already present — matching Oracle text
// that never says "target". "toughness" defaults to 0 when absent
// (matching the printed "+X/+0") but is left alone if an earlier resolver
// already set it, so a differently-worded "+X/+X" variant of this shape
// could reuse the fold without losing its toughness value. A no-op
// (returns effectValue/targets unchanged) when the key is absent, or when
// eventCard or source is nil.
func (gs *GameState) resolvePumpReferentSpellManaValue(effectValue map[string]interface{}, targets []Target, eventCard *Card, source *Card) (map[string]interface{}, []Target) {
	if effectValue == nil {
		return effectValue, targets
	}
	from, _ := effectValue["power_from"].(string)
	if from == "greatest_power_among_controlled_creatures" {
		if source == nil {
			return effectValue, targets
		}
		best := 0
		for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
			if c == nil || !c.IsCreature() || c.Controller != source.Controller || c.BasePower == nil {
				continue
			}
			if *c.BasePower > best {
				best = *c.BasePower
			}
		}
		effectValue["power"] = best
		if _, ok := effectValue["toughness"]; !ok {
			effectValue["toughness"] = 0
		}
		return effectValue, []Target{{IsCard: true, CardID: source.ID}}
	}
	// discarded_card_mana_value (ticket
	// engine.auto-pump-until-eot-dynamic-mana-value-149544d1aa, Spellbound
	// Dragon class: "Whenever this creature attacks, draw a card, then
	// discard a card. This creature gets +X/+0 until end of turn, where X
	// is the discarded card's mana value.") reuses this exact fold:
	// eventCard is the discarded card (the same StackObject.EventCard
	// referent resolveRecipientFromEventCard's mana_value amount_source
	// reads above for the analogous Hellhole Rats damage case), and "this
	// creature" is source — never a chosen target, matching the untargeted
	// referent_spell_mana_value sibling this shares its fold with.
	if from != "referent_spell_mana_value" && from != "discarded_card_mana_value" {
		return effectValue, targets
	}
	if eventCard == nil || source == nil {
		return effectValue, targets
	}
	effectValue["power"] = eventCard.ManaCost.CMC()
	if _, ok := effectValue["toughness"]; !ok {
		effectValue["toughness"] = 0
	}
	return effectValue, []Target{{IsCard: true, CardID: source.ID}}
}

// resolvePumpSubtypeCountAmount implements the
// pump_until_eot_self_dynamic_amount primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-pump-until-eot-self-dynamic-amount-bb1ddfc01a, Vile Deacon
// class: "Whenever this creature attacks, it gets +X/+X until end of turn,
// where X is the number of Clerics on the battlefield."). Sibling of
// resolvePumpReferentSpellManaValue above, but the dynamic value comes from
// a BOARD-STATE COUNT read fresh at resolution time
// (EffectValue["count_subtype"]), not a referent event card. When
// EffectValue["power_from"] == "count_subtype_battlefield", this counts
// every creature on the battlefield (either player's — Oracle text says
// "on the battlefield", not "you control") whose SubTypes include the
// named subtype and sets "power" to that count, defaulting "toughness" to
// the same count (+X/+X) when absent. A no-op (returns effectValue
// unchanged) when the key is absent, effectValue or source is nil, or
// count_subtype is empty — so a literal numeric pump amount, or a pump
// keyed by a different power_from convention (e.g.
// "referent_spell_mana_value" above), is never affected by this fold.
func (gs *GameState) resolvePumpSubtypeCountAmount(effectValue map[string]interface{}, source *Card) map[string]interface{} {
	if effectValue == nil || source == nil {
		return effectValue
	}
	from, _ := effectValue["power_from"].(string)
	if from != "count_subtype_battlefield" {
		return effectValue
	}
	subtype, _ := effectValue["count_subtype"].(string)
	if subtype == "" {
		return effectValue
	}
	count := gs.countCreaturesWithSubtypeOnBattlefield(subtype)
	effectValue["power"] = count
	if _, ok := effectValue["toughness"]; !ok {
		effectValue["toughness"] = count
	}
	return effectValue
}

// countCreaturesWithSubtypeOnBattlefield counts every creature on the
// battlefield whose SubTypes include subtype, read fresh on every call so a
// board-state change between two resolutions (a Cleric entering or leaving)
// is picked up without any caching — the amount is never resolved once and
// memoized at parse/cast time.
func (gs *GameState) countCreaturesWithSubtypeOnBattlefield(subtype string) int {
	count := 0
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil || !c.IsCreature() {
			continue
		}
		for _, st := range c.SubTypes {
			if st == subtype {
				count++
				break
			}
		}
	}
	return count
}

// resolvePumpTargetedSpellManaValue implements the
// amount_source_spell_mana_value primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-amount-source-spell-mana-value-497cd8b198, Livaan, Cultist of
// Tiamat class: "Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, target creature
// gets +X/+0 until end of turn, where X is that spell's mana value.").
// eventCard is the triggering cast spell (StackObject.EventCard, the same
// referent field resolvePumpReferentSpellManaValue reads above). Keyed by
// EffectValue["power_from"]=="referent_spell_mana_value_targeted" (closed,
// single value, deliberately distinct from the untargeted
// "referent_spell_mana_value" key so neither fold fires on the other's
// shape); sets "power" to that spell's mana value (Card.ManaCost.CMC(),
// the same read resolvePumpReferentSpellManaValue uses) and defaults
// "toughness" to 0 when absent, matching the printed "+X/+0" — but unlike
// that sibling resolver, targets are left untouched since the Oracle text
// says "target creature", a player-chosen target distinct from source. A
// no-op (returns effectValue unchanged) when the key is absent or
// eventCard is nil, so a fixed numeric pump amount (keyed by a literal
// "power"/"toughness" with no "power_from" at all) is never affected.
func (gs *GameState) resolvePumpTargetedSpellManaValue(effectValue map[string]interface{}, eventCard *Card) map[string]interface{} {
	if effectValue == nil {
		return effectValue
	}
	from, _ := effectValue["power_from"].(string)
	if from != "referent_spell_mana_value_targeted" || eventCard == nil {
		return effectValue
	}
	effectValue["power"] = eventCard.ManaCost.CMC()
	if _, ok := effectValue["toughness"]; !ok {
		effectValue["toughness"] = 0
	}
	return effectValue
}

// resolveSelfCopyOnCastAmount implements the
// triggered_self_copy_on_cast primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-triggered-self-copy-on-cast-0963aa4e59, Photon Blast Barrage
// class): "When you cast this spell, copy it X times." executeCopyEffect and
// executeSelfCopyOnCastEffect (advanced_abilities.go) treat "declining the
// optional retarget" (Rule 706.10) as always legal, so the copies keep the
// original spell's targets — the same conservative-subset behavior
// copy_effect.go documents for every other copy shape in this file; this
// resolver only closes the "amount" gap, not the retarget gap.
// executeSelfCopyOnCastEffect only reads EffectValue["count"] (a static
// number) or ["count_var"]=="commander_casts", but a printed X reaches this
// dispatch as a generic EffectValue["amount"] via the amount_count/
// amount_from folds above (e.g. resolveAmountFrom's "x_value" source for an
// announced {X}) — this bridges that generic "amount" into "count" right
// before the executor runs, on a copied map so the original StackObject's
// EffectValue is never mutated. A no-op when "count"/"count_var" is already
// present (the two pre-existing shapes) or "amount" is absent.
func (gs *GameState) resolveSelfCopyOnCastAmount(effectValue map[string]interface{}) map[string]interface{} {
	if effectValue == nil {
		return effectValue
	}
	if _, ok := effectValue["count"]; ok {
		return effectValue
	}
	if _, ok := effectValue["count_var"]; ok {
		return effectValue
	}
	amount, ok := effectValue["amount"]
	if !ok {
		return effectValue
	}
	merged := make(map[string]interface{}, len(effectValue)+1)
	for k, v := range effectValue {
		merged[k] = v
	}
	merged["count"] = amount
	return merged
}

// executeTapUntapSharedCreatureTypeReferent implements the
// tap_untap_shared_creature_type_referent primitive (Faces of the Past
// class: "Whenever a creature dies, tap all untapped creatures that share
// a creature type with it or untap all tapped creatures that share a
// creature type with it."): the referent is eventCard — the triggering
// event's subject — never an explicit chosen target.
// EffectValue["mode"]=="untap" untaps every battlefield creature sharing
// at least one entry in SubTypes with eventCard; any other value (default
// "tap") taps them instead. All matches are applied in one battlefield-
// wide sweep, not through the single-target tap primitive. No-ops when
// there is no event card to read a referent from. v1 approximation:
// matches on shared SubTypes alone (no separate creature-type gate),
// mirroring other documented v1 scopes in this file.
func (gs *GameState) executeTapUntapSharedCreatureTypeReferent(effectValue map[string]interface{}, eventCard *Card) {
	if eventCard == nil || len(eventCard.SubTypes) == 0 {
		return
	}
	mode, _ := effectValue["mode"].(string)
	if mode != "untap" {
		mode = "tap"
	}
	referentTypes := make(map[string]bool, len(eventCard.SubTypes))
	for _, st := range eventCard.SubTypes {
		referentTypes[st] = true
	}
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.Cards {
		if c == nil {
			continue
		}
		shared := false
		for _, st := range c.SubTypes {
			if referentTypes[st] {
				shared = true
				break
			}
		}
		if !shared {
			continue
		}
		c.Tapped = mode == "tap"
	}
}

// executeUntapDynamicXTargetLands implements the targeted_effect_dynamic_x_count
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-targeted-effect-dynamic-x-count-d629ddac55,
// Candelabra of Tawnos class: "{X}, {T}: Untap X target lands."). Untaps
// every land named in targets — no fewer than X (a fixed single-target
// untap) and no more (an untap-all-lands sweep): the exact count is
// enforced upstream, when the activating player is required to choose
// targets equal to the X they announced/paid for the ability's {X} cost.
// A target whose card is missing from the battlefield or isn't a land is
// skipped rather than failing the whole effect (Rule 601.2c: an illegal
// individual target doesn't invalidate the others).
func (gs *GameState) executeUntapDynamicXTargetLands(targets []Target) error {
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.Cards {
			if c != nil && c.ID == t.CardID && c.Type == TypeLand {
				c.Tapped = false
				break
			}
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeDestroyTargetWithMaxManaValue implements the
// destroy_target_creature_with_max_mana_value primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-destroy-target-creature-with-max-mana-value-a65c60f7c9, Feed
// the Cauldron class: "Destroy target creature with mana value 3 or
// less."). Unlike the plain single-target destroy (DestroyTarget, whose
// only checks are targeting-time), this adds a resolution-time numeric
// ceiling on the target's mana value — mirrors
// executeDestroyTargetUnlessLifeAtLeast's "read a threshold out of
// effectValue, check it per target at resolution" idiom just below. A
// target missing from the battlefield, or whose ManaCost.CMC() exceeds
// EffectValue["max_mana_value"], is skipped rather than failing the whole
// effect (Rule 601.2c partial-legality). This does not touch DestroyTarget
// or any existing "destroy" dispatch case, so the plain
// creature/tapped_creature/attacking_or_blocking_creature/mass-destroy
// shapes are unaffected.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyTargetWithMaxManaValue(targets []Target, effectValue map[string]interface{}) error {
	maxManaValue := getIntValue(effectValue, "max_mana_value", -1)
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		var card *Card
		for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
			if c != nil && c.ID == t.CardID {
				card = c
				break
			}
		}
		if card == nil {
			continue
		}
		if maxManaValue >= 0 && card.ManaCost.CMC() > maxManaValue {
			continue
		}
		if err := DestroyTarget(t, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeDestroyTargetWithFilter implements the destroy_target_with_filter
// primitive (ticket engine.auto-creature-with-power-n-or-greater-ac0c378c29,
// Skywhaler's Shot class): "Destroy target creature with power 3 or
// greater." Unlike executeDestroyTargetWithMaxManaValue's bespoke CMC
// ceiling just above, this reuses matchesPermanentFilter (gamestate.go) —
// the SAME filter vocabulary "destroy all X" mass effects already use,
// including its massFilterCmpRe power/toughness/mv threshold suffix
// ("creature_power_gte_3") — so a single-target destroy can be gated by a
// numeric power predicate without inventing a new one-off comparison. A
// target missing from the battlefield, or failing the filter, is skipped
// rather than failing the whole effect (Rule 601.2c partial-legality),
// matching the sibling destroy-with-a-condition executors below. Plain
// "destroy target creature"/"destroy target artifact"/etc. never call this
// executor, so they remain unaffected (negative example: no power
// predicate is added where none was requested).
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyTargetWithFilter(targets []Target, filter string) error {
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil {
			continue
		}
		if !matchesPermanentFilter(card, filter) {
			continue
		}
		if err := DestroyTarget(t, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeExileTargetWithMaxManaValue applies composable type, controller,
// and maximum mana-value filters to battlefield targets.
func (gs *GameState) executeExileTargetWithMaxManaValue(targets []Target, filter string, controllerIdx int, scope string, maxManaValue int) error {
	filter = strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace(filter))
	scope = strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace(scope))

	for _, target := range targets {
		if !target.IsCard || target.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(target.CardID)
		if err != nil || card == nil {
			continue
		}
		if scope == "you" && card.Controller != controllerIdx {
			continue
		}
		if scope == "opponent" && card.Controller == controllerIdx {
			continue
		}
		if filter != "" && !matchesPermanentFilter(card, filter) {
			continue
		}
		if maxManaValue >= 0 && card.ManaCost.CMC() > maxManaValue {
			continue
		}

		removed, err := gs.Battlefield.RemoveCard(card.ID)
		if err != nil || removed == nil {
			continue
		}
		gs.EffectManager.RemoveBySource(removed)
		gs.UnregisterCardAbilities(removed)
		if removed.Owner >= 0 && removed.Owner < len(gs.Players) {
			gs.Players[removed.Owner].Exile.AddCard(removed)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeDestroyTargetDealtDamageThisTurn implements the
// filter_dealt_damage_this_turn primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-filter-dealt-damage-this-turn-acfa9c82ee, Manticore class):
// "destroy target creature an opponent controls that was dealt damage this
// turn." Rather than a bespoke one-off check like the numeric ceiling above,
// this reuses matchesPermanentFilterCtl (gamestate.go) so the new
// "_dealt_damage_this_turn" filter suffix composes with the SAME
// controller/type buckets every other destroy-with-a-filter shape already
// uses (e.g. filter "creature_dealt_damage_this_turn_opp_ctrl"). A target
// missing from the battlefield, or failing the filter, is skipped rather
// than failing the whole effect (Rule 601.2c partial-legality), matching the
// sibling destroy-with-a-condition executors above and below.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyTargetDealtDamageThisTurn(targets []Target, filter string, controllerIdx int) error {
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil {
			continue
		}
		if !matchesPermanentFilterCtl(card, filter, controllerIdx) {
			continue
		}
		if err := DestroyTarget(t, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeDestroyTargetReferentControllerFilter implements the
// trigger_referent_controller_filter primitive (ticket
// engine.auto-trigger-referent-controller-filter-b764b9eb7d, Soltari
// Visionary class): "Whenever this creature deals damage to a player,
// destroy target enchantment that player controls." Unlike a static
// "you control"/"an opponent controls" filter, the controller here is the
// anaphoric referent introduced by the trigger condition itself — the
// player this permanent just dealt damage to (eventPlayer, the same kind
// of referent ResolveTriggerReferentPlayerController resolves above for
// the tap-lands primitive) — never the ability's own controller and never
// an explicitly chosen target. A target missing from the battlefield, not
// an enchantment, or not controlled by eventPlayer is skipped rather than
// failing the whole effect (Rule 601.2c partial-legality), matching
// executeDestroyTargetDealtDamageThisTurn's idiom just above. eventPlayer
// < 0 (no referent identified) skips every target, failing closed rather
// than destroying an unintended permanent.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyTargetReferentControllerFilter(targets []Target, eventPlayer int) error {
	if eventPlayer < 0 {
		return nil
	}
	for _, t := range targets {
		if !t.IsCard || t.CardID == "" {
			continue
		}
		card, err := gs.Battlefield.GetCard(t.CardID)
		if err != nil {
			continue
		}
		if card.Controller != eventPlayer {
			continue
		}
		isEnchantment := false
		for _, ty := range card.Types {
			if ty == "Enchantment" {
				isEnchantment = true
				break
			}
		}
		if !isEnchantment {
			continue
		}
		if err := DestroyTarget(t, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeDestroyEachDealtDamageThisTurn implements the
// filter_dealt_damage_this_turn primitive in its MASS shape (ticket
// engine.auto-filter-dealt-damage-this-turn-8001dad946, Swift Demise class:
// "destroy each creature you don't control that was dealt damage this
// turn"). Unlike executeDestroyTargetDealtDamageThisTurn just above (which
// operates on a fixed set of chosen targets), this sweeps the whole
// battlefield and destroys every permanent matching the filter, so it
// reuses the SAME matchesPermanentFilterCtl controller/type buckets plus the
// "_dealt_damage_this_turn" suffix (gamestate.go) rather than inventing a
// new match path. filter already carries the controller restriction
// (e.g. "creature_dealt_damage_this_turn_opp_ctrl"); controllerIdx is the
// ability's controller. Victims are snapshotted before any destruction so a
// simultaneous sweep can't affect whether a later permanent still matches.
func (gs *GameState) executeDestroyEachDealtDamageThisTurn(filter string, controllerIdx int) error {
	victims := []Target{}
	for _, c := range gs.Battlefield.GetAll() {
		if c == nil {
			continue
		}
		if !matchesPermanentFilterCtl(c, filter, controllerIdx) {
			continue
		}
		victims = append(victims, Target{IsCard: true, CardID: c.ID})
	}
	for _, t := range victims {
		if err := DestroyTarget(t, gs); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// executeDamageEqualToDamageAlreadyDealt implements "~ deals damage to
// target creature equal to the damage already dealt to it this turn"
// (Whipkeeper class, ticket 8109732332). The amount is read from the
// TARGET's own Card.Damage field — marked damage (Rule 120.3), zeroed every
// cleanup step by ExecuteCleanupStep — so it already IS "damage dealt to it
// this turn" with no separate tracker needed. This is per-target (unlike a
// flat "amount" field) and distinct from the adjacent fixed_numeric_damage
// and "equal to its power" atoms, which never read the target's own marked
// damage. Delegates the actual dealing to executeDamageEffect (one target
// at a time, with that target's own amount) so prevention shields, damage
// multipliers, event dispatch, and the sequence "dealt" tally all still
